Document 002: 10 questions for China in 2027

### Page 1

2026 has been a year of slowing growth and increasing
divergence within China's economy. We saw the gap between
external and domestic demand, as well as between the new
economy and the old economy, widen signiﬁcantly. Here, we
look ahead to the key questions facing China in 2027
Lynn Song
Beijing, China
1) Will China’s growth target be lowered
again?
Despite a growing emphasis on the quality of growth rather than
just the pace, China’s annual economic target-setting exercise in

March remains one of the year's most important events. The GDP
growth target and ﬁscal quotas tend to determine the level of
policy support needed in the year ahead.
China very rarely misses its growth target. Since 1991, it has fallen
signiﬁcantly short of target only once – after the widespread
pandemic lockdowns in 2022.

### Page 2

In 2026, lowering the growth target from “around 5%” to 4.5-5.0%
set the stage for slower activity and less urgency for stimulus than
in previous years. We expect the target to be achieved, but growth
will likely be toward the low end of the range.
The key question for 2027 is whether we’ll see the growth target
soften further. China’s economy has seen growing imbalances over
the past year. Next year’s growth will rely heavily on external
demand and new-economy drivers. Uncertainty about whether

external demand and the AI-driven tech capex boom can continue
could aﬀect overall growth.
We expect a slight softening of the growth target to “around 4.5%”.
This would allow for decent policy ﬂexibility while keeping the long-
term challenges in mind. Our calculations show China will need to
grow at an average of around 4% per year to achieve the 2030
(RMB170tr) and 2035 (RMB207tr) targets set by Premier Li Qiang
and President Xi Jinping, respectively. A target of “around 4.5%”

gives China some buﬀer room if growth slows more signiﬁcantly
going forward.
Accordingly, we expect growth to come in at 4.5% year-on-year in
2027, broadly in line with our 4.6% forecast for 2026. This will
maintain China’s role as a key driver of global growth. China is
expected to contribute somewhere between a ﬁfth and a quarter of
global growth over the next ﬁve years.
China's growth target may be adjusted to
"around 4.5%" in 2027

### Page 3

2) Will external demand and tech drive
growth again?
We observed two major K-shaped trends in 2026: the divergence
between strong external demand and weak domestic demand, and
the split between the tech-led new economy and the old economy.
We expect export growth to moderate but remain relatively strong
next year, with high-single-digit increases in exports. While things
are likely to normalise a bit after an extraordinarily strong year in
2026, external demand will still remain one of China's key growth

engines.
First, strong export performance in 2026 will create a challenging
base eﬀect to follow in 2027. The run-up in energy and technology
prices that underpinned growth is also unlikely to be repeated,
reducing the scope for a similar boost next year.
Second, uncertainty could return in 2027 after what has been a
relatively calm year in terms of trade developments following a
roller-coaster 2025.
The China-US trade truce at the time of writing is set to expire in

January 2027. While our base case is a further extension of this
agreement, risks of re-escalation can’t be ruled out, particularly
as China’s trade surplus widened 12% YoY to $233.8bn through
the ﬁrst three quarters of 2026. The ‘blue wave’ election
outcome in the US could lead to gridlock and prompt US
President Donald Trump to focus on areas where he can
exercise executive power, such as trade policy.

### Page 4

Possible trade tensions with the EU are also worth considering.
ASEAN has overtaken the US as China’s largest export
destination. The EU remains second, representing 14-15% of
China’s total exports. China’s trade surplus with the EU surged
26.1% YoY to $277.4bn in the ﬁrst three quarters of 2026. After
trade talks in October, China and the EU agreed to a 16-point
plan to improve trade ties. The plan includes a cap on exports
of hybrid vehicles into the EU at 2025 levels. Almost

immediately, calls emerged to extend the quotas to other
industries. Almost immediately after, there were additional
calls to extend these quotas to other industries. While we
believe there’s plenty of room for win-win cooperation, there’s
also risk of trade friction increasing in the year ahead.
On the tech side, 2026 marked an acceleration of the capex cycle
and AI boom. Hi-tech investment was one of the few areas that saw
positive investment growth in 2026, and tech-related exports and

industrial production also outperformed on the year.
However, we also saw several notable market pullbacks along with
rising fears of an AI bubble bursting. One of the biggest questions
for not just China but the global economy at large is whether
impressive tech growth continues into 2027.
At this juncture, the AI-driven investment boom looks likely to
continue into 2027, with many companies still reporting order
books full through 2027. We think the current engines of growth will

moderate but continue to drive growth into 2027. To achieve stable
growth, other parts of the economy might need to pick up the
slack. Our next question deals with whether domestic demand can
step up to the plate.
Technology products and export
diversiﬁcation continue to support external
demand

### Page 5

3) Can domestic demand carry more of the
load?
Anyone following Chinese policymakers' messaging will have
noticed a recurring theme in recent years: the need to increase the
role of domestic demand in driving growth.
China’s 15th Five-Year Plan places building a strong domestic
market among its top priorities, alongside industrial
modernisation, tech self-reliance, and the digital economy. Yet
while the latter three have been obvious recipients of policy backing

and resources, the push to strengthen domestic demand has often
appeared less tangible. These categories are clearly policy
favourites and receive more resources.
However, 2026 was one of the weakest years for domestic demand
in recent history. Over the ﬁrst three quarters, China’s retail sales
grew just 1.1% YoY ytd.
In our view, several factors played a major role in weak
consumption growth in 2026:
Trade-in programme turned from tailwind into headwind.

China’s ﬂagship policy to boost consumption was the trade-in
programme. It front-loaded consumption by oﬀering consumer
subsidies for targeted products across categories including
autos, household appliances, furniture, and communication
devices. The programme's scale fell slightly in 2026, which
along with previous front-loading clearly dragged retail sales
growth, with household appliance, furniture, and auto sales
down year on year.
Consumer conﬁdence ﬂatlined after a modest recovery in

2025. Conﬁdence peaked in February 2026, but pulled back

### Page 6

afterwards. Low wage growth, combined with a negative
wealth eﬀect from falling equity and property prices, was the
main culprit for soft conﬁdence. Weak conﬁdence continued to
reduce discretionary consumption categories for most of the
year.
The peak and subsequent drop of gold prices dragged gold &
jewellery sales. Gold prices peaked in January. The subsequent
bust signiﬁcantly reduced retail interest in buying gold.
Heading into 2027, we expect a modest recovery in consumption,

with potential upside for a larger recovery.
Drag from trade-in programme should be smaller. The
pullback we saw from the household appliances, furniture, and
auto categories should be smaller in 2027. It’s too early to
expect signiﬁcant replacement demand for appliances and
furniture, but auto sales could begin to see some replacement
demand from the ﬁrst wave of EV sales. This could reduce the
category's drag. However, given the strength of communication

appliance sales in 2026, this category is ripe for a 2027
pullback.
Policy support for domestic demand could pick up, especially
if the external demand picture worsens. While it seems
reasonable to expect resources to remain concentrated toward
tech thanks to the AI race, supporting domestic demand could
become more important if we see signs of the external demand
engine slowing in 2027. Longer term, increasing domestic
demand is necessary to achieve more sustainable growth.

The big wildcard is how consumer conﬁdence fares next year. The
main factors will likely be wage growth and asset price
performance. We’ve seen some encouraging signs that property
prices are beginning to bottom out in tier 1 and a few tier 2 cities.
This could start to translate into a conﬁdence recovery. While wage
growth has remained slow amid a weak job market, a recovery of
corporate proﬁts could potentially translate into a recovery if the
trend holds.

### Page 7

Weak consumer conﬁdence remains the
main constraint on domestic demand
4) Has China successfully escaped
deﬂation?
China’s struggle with deﬂation in the past few years triggered
signiﬁcant debate about whether China was headed down the
same path as Japan’s Lost Decades. Wealth destruction from the
property market bust, aggressive price competition, and poor
domestic demand contributed to three straight years of near-zero
inﬂation. It also threatened to put China into a negative feedback

loop of falling prices and proﬁtability, lower wage growth, and
weaker consumption and investment.
However, in 2026 we ﬁnally saw a more convincing uptick in
inﬂation, ending the year above 0% for the ﬁrst time since 2022.
We think the worst of the deﬂationary pressure may be behind us.
Looking ahead to 2027, several factors are likely to continue
shaping the inﬂation outlook.
Food and housing have been the two biggest drags on inﬂation
over the past year, together accounting for more than half of

China's CPI basket. The drag from food should gradually fade as
pork prices eventually stabilise. Pork has started to see sequential
increases starting in the second half of 2026. Abundant domestic
supplies of fresh vegetables and fruits should continue thanks to
greenhouse expansion, but likely won't repeat the same
deﬂationary impact next year.

### Page 8

Rent may continue to drag on inﬂation until we see a broader
bottom in the property market. Rent has been at -0.6% YoY since
April 2026.
This year’s primary inﬂation driver was energy prices, as fallout
from the Iran war hit the global economy. Energy costs are a
wildcard in 2027. Our house forecasts Brent averaging $81/bbl in
2027, which should keep inﬂation elevated after higher prices in
2026.
Tech prices, by contrast, surged in 2026 as the AI boom drove

heavy demand and price hikes across many tech supply chains.
This was most evident in the communication appliances
component of China's CPI basket, where prices rose 6.3% YoY in the
ﬁrst three quarters.
Furthermore, China’s PPI inﬂation surged in 2026. Producer prices
rose amid higher raw material and other input prices. Companies
have been reluctant to pass higher costs on to consumers amid
subdued conﬁdence. But if input costs remain elevated into next

year, maintaining that restraint may become increasingly diﬃcult.
Overall, we expect China’s CPI inﬂation to move a little higher next
year, to 1.1% YoY. This level is a sweet spot for China, staying a
comfortable distance from deﬂation while avoiding the high
inﬂation pressure many other economies are currently facing. At
this level, we don’t expect inﬂation to constrain monetary easing if
needed.
Inﬂation has turned higher, but the recovery
remains uneven

### Page 9

5) Will the drag from the property market
ﬁnally fade?
Barring an astounding end-of-year turnaround, property prices will
have fallen for a ﬁfth consecutive year by the time 2026 wraps up.
China's property downturn has evolved considerably over the past
few years. Fears of a catastrophic systemic crisis have largely
faded, and developer defaults have become less frequent. Yet home
prices still haven’t found a clear ﬂoor, and the pessimism unleashed

by the housing slump remains deeply embedded in household
sentiment. The property market remains a major overhang on the
economy.
Building sales and real estate ﬁxed-asset investment continued to
see steep declines, down -13.9% YoY and -20.6%, respectively.
The indirect impact of the property bust is harder to measure, but
it’s widespread and arguably even more signiﬁcant.
Consumer conﬁdence has been weak since the property
downturn began, and the recovery has been slow and uneven

since.
Adjacent industries have been impacted. Retail sales of
furniture (-4.7%), household appliance sales (-4.9%), and
building & decoration materials (-9.7%) are in contraction this
year. Industrial production data shows signiﬁcant weakness in
property-related categories such as cement (-8.6%), ﬂat glass
(-7.0%), and steel (-2.0%) through the third quarter.
Land sales traditionally have been a major source of local
government revenue. This dried up as demand plummeted

amid the property bust. Local-government revenue from
transfers of state-owned land-use rights fell from CNY8.7tn in
2021 to CNY4.15tn in 2025, and looks to have fallen further in
2026. The consequence of this is that local governments are
also tightening their belts in terms of spending.
Falling home and rental prices are contributing to deﬂationary
pressure.
Is the drag from the property sector going to fade in 2027?

### Page 10

In our view, two things need to happen before we see a property
sector turnaround. First, we need to see signs that price
stabilisation is becoming more entrenched. This year has delivered
the most encouraging evidence since the property downturn
began, with prices in tier-one cities broadly stabilising or rising for
much of the year. If that trend persists and spreads to more tier-two
and tier-three cities, it could mark the bottom of the current cycle.

For now, the 70-city average property price index has yet to
establish a bottom.
Second, property inventories remain elevated, reaching a new
record high in early 2026 before dropping later in the year. Until
these return to more normal levels, it's diﬃcult to expect signiﬁcant
new property investment.
Policy support for the property market has expanded modestly in
2026, including a mortgage subsidy for ﬁrst-time buyers, further
loosening of purchase restrictions across the country, and

continuing with urban renewal projects.
We also saw some structural reforms. These include tightening
restrictions on the property pre-sale model, such as requiring
developers to reach a certain stage of completion before pre-sales
and requiring funds to enter a supervised account. These steps
make the operating environment more challenging for developers
but should boost buyer conﬁdence.
With further support for the property market, we think we could see

a smaller drag from the sector in 2027. If price stabilisation
continues, it would be a big step, removing a major weight on
household and investment conﬁdence. That said, with inventories
still high and developers limiting new projects, real estate
investment will likely remain a headwind next year.
High inventories and falling prices continue
to weigh on the property market

### Page 11

6) What can we expect from policymakers
in 2027?
With the 2026 growth target lowered and attention consumed by
the AI race, it is perhaps unsurprising that policy support for the
broader economy has been underwhelming this year. We expect a
generally supportive policy environment for the year ahead, with
the possibility for more aggressive policy rollout to bolster the
domestic economy if we see trade tensions pick back up next year.
On ﬁscal policy, we expect the key ﬁscal targets to remain fairly

stable in 2027.
The ﬁscal deﬁcit target is likely to remain around 4% of GDP for
a third straight year. Adjustments to this target would be
primarily for signalling purposes rather than to actively
constrain policy.
Our base case for bond issuance targets is that they remain
little changed, around RMB 4.4tn for special local government
bonds and RMB 1.3tn for ultra-long-term bonds. Given the
limited appetite for using approved quotas in 2026 amid the

drop in investment, it's possible that targets could be lowered.
Our base case is for targets to stay high to increase ﬂexibility.
On monetary policy, we expect the People’s Bank of China to buck
global trends and maintain an easing bias. Inﬂation remains well
below target, the labour market remains fairly soft, growth has
been decelerating, and the CNY has been strengthening. Conditions
are mostly suitable for further easing, with the key exception being

bank net interest margins are already at historical lows, and
policymakers have thus far preferred targeted easing instead.
Below are our key calls for the PBOC next year:

### Page 12

We look for a single 10bp rate cut to the benchmark rate. The
PBOC has resisted broad-based rate cuts, mindful of low
banking-sector margins and the fact that many major central
banks have remained in hiking mode.
Targeted measures to support selected industries look likely
to continue. Re-lending programmes, interest rate subsidies,
and risk-sharing facilities may remain the main tools for
allocating money to areas of need.
PBOC benchmark rate shift will improve monetary policy

transmission. The PBOC formalised a shift away from the 7-
day reverse repo rate to the overnight reverse repo rate starting
2 November 2026. We expect this shift to improve monetary
policy transmission, enable more precise day-to-day liquidity
management, and move China’s framework closer to global
peers.
At the sector level, we expect policy priorities in 2027 to remain
closely aligned with those laid out in the Five-Year Plan, with
industrial modernisation and technological self-reliance continuing

to attract the lion's share of attention and resources.
Given recent communications, we may also see more concerted
eﬀorts to build out domestic demand. An October National
Development and Reform Commission (NDRC) news conference
oﬀered some food for thought, promising to “strengthen inclusive
policies that reach consumers directly” alongside existing strategies
to expand and upgrade goods such as new energy vehicles and
smart products. One limitation of recent consumption-support

measures is that, while trade-in programmes have successfully
brought forward spending, they have done little to lift household
conﬁdence. The next step may be to recalibrate policy to make
consumers feel genuinely better oﬀ.
Support for the property market should also continue to spread the
initial recovery seen in the core cities nationwide.
Policy will remain supportive, but broad
stimulus is unlikely

### Page 13

7) Will CNY two-way moves return after
2026's gains?
We set an initial forecast band for the USDCNY in 2027 of 6.40-6.80,
and our 2027 year-end forecast set at 6.50.
2026 marked another strong year for the CNY, as the appreciation
momentum started in 2025 and continued into 2026 despite what
has largely been a strong dollar backdrop. Generally, appreciation
has been stronger than what most market participants had
expected at the start of the year.

As the numbers above imply, our base case has appreciation
continuing. Our key reasons are listed below:
We continue to hear a very strong consensus among market
participants for a moderate pace of appreciation to continue.
While this raises risks of a sharper reversal, it also remains
diﬃcult to bet against.
Exporters continue to sell into upticks of the USDCNY, creating a
convincing environment for CNY appreciation to continue.
Considering the foreign currency left abroad over the past few

years, and incoming foreign currency receivables with China’s
trade surplus set to hit a new record high in 2026 and remain
strong in 2027, the stage is set for further CNY appreciation.
PBOC has signalled it has neither the need nor intention to use
currency depreciation to gain advantages in trade. This
suggests that there won't be a push to depreciate the currency
even if the current trade boom cools oﬀ.
CNY strength has thus far been without the support of equity

market inﬂows. Chinese markets have been an underperformer.
Your privacy is important

### Page 14

But if there's some mean reversion or a bull market reversal,
this could further support CNY strength.
However, we don't think 2027 will see a repeat of this relatively
simple straight line appreciation from April 2025 to the time of
writing. Two-way ﬂuctuation looks likely to return, and the recent
CNY appreciation bias in markets raises the risk for a stronger
reversal, even if the PBOC acts to limit excessive volatility.
US-China yield spreads remain near-record highs. This

correlation weakened in 2026 as investors became worried
about missing out on further appreciation, and as the spread
narrowed to start the year. However, a hawkish shift as the Iran
war broke out has widened the yield spread to new highs. This
should eventually tempt investors.
PBOC could step in if the appreciation trend goes too far. In an
October policy statement, the PBOC warned against one-sided
and procyclical behaviour in the FX market, reﬂecting concern

that market participants can reinforce an exchange-rate move
through a herding eﬀect. In early November, the PBOC raised
the foreign-exchange deposit reserve requirement from 4% to
6%. It was widely seen as a warning against excessive yuan
appreciation and one-sided speculative positioning,
discouraging this behaviour by draining foreign-currency
liquidity from the banking system.
Outward direct investment looks to be closing a year with an
uncharacteristic year-on-year decline after seeing double-digit

growth for 5 of the past 6 years. A recovery of ODI demand
could add selling pressure to the CNY.
CNY appreciation should continue, but with
greater two-way volatility

### Page 15

8) Are CGBs the biggest asymmetric risk to
Chinese markets?
China’s government bond yields bucked global trends in 2026,
falling despite a surge in yields elsewhere in the world amid rising
inﬂation and debt.
The falling yields have been a double-edged sword. On one hand,
low yields have allowed the government to issue debt cheaply, an
important beneﬁt as China brings shadow-banking debt onto
oﬃcial balance sheets, and as debt levels remain high. However, the

low yields have also become a symbol of weak investor conﬁdence
and a factor cited by those arguing that China is seeing entrenched
pessimism and on a path of “Japanisation.”
China’s government bond yields have generally trended downward
since 2020, with pockets of volatility throughout. Several key
factors have contributed to this long bull market:
Investor risk appetite remains abnormally soft. Although we
would argue that China exited “peak pessimism” in 2024,

many market participants still maintain very conservative
asset allocations. Equity and property markets continue to
underperform, and demand for risk-free assets remains huge.
Limited credit demand has led to rising bank holdings of CGBs.
Bank holdings of CGBs have nearly quadrupled over the past
decade and stood at RMB 78.6tn at the end of 3Q26.
Many remain trapped in a deﬂationary mindset. China has
taken steps to move out of deﬂation in 2026, thanks to higher

energy prices that boosted core inﬂation. Yet the deﬂationary
mindset persists for many, contributing to a willingness to
accept such low yields.

### Page 16

Tighter restrictions on outbound investment may be
preventing investors from investing in higher-yielding bonds
abroad. China’s yields are now signiﬁcantly lower than most
major economies, but domestic investors still face limitations
on how much they can move abroad to capitalise on this
spread. Capital controls remain a major factor in how yields
can stay so low.
We think there’s a solid case for bond yields to turn around in 2027.
A slight normalisation of risk appetite would be suﬃcient to

cause a major shift. A return to a more balanced asset
allocation would be a catalyst for risk asset rallies and a bond
market pullback. Banks are investing in bonds instead of
lending thanks to unusually weak investment appetite. A
reversion to the mean would be the biggest catalyst for a bond
selloﬀ.
China’s long-term growth and inﬂation outlook don’t match
the current yields. Current yields imply a deﬂationary,
stagnant environment, while most economists expect

moderating but still solid growth and low but positive inﬂation.
Time will tell which side is right, but bond yields suggest
markets are pricing in a signiﬁcantly more downbeat outlook
than what the data is showing.
Bond issuance is expected to remain high, and debt levels
remain elevated. As we discussed earlier in this report, China’s
ﬁscal policy stance is likely to remain accommodative. Another
year of high bond issuance should add some supply pressure

on the CGB market.
Higher global yields make Chinese yields even less attractive
by comparison. China’s bond yields are now among the lowest
of major economies. Capital controls will limit how far investors
can chase higher yields, but an asset allocation shift from
domestic to global bonds could still play a role.
Policymakers may start to worry about the implications of
low long-end yields, and push back if the bull market
continues. Recall that back in 2024, the PBOC had repeatedly

intervened to keep the 10-year CGB yield above 2%. While
these eﬀorts were discontinued, market rumours at the end of

October suggested that policymakers had asked Chinese
ﬁnancial institutions not to add duration in the bond market.
We look for 10-year CGB yields to rise to 1.9% by the end of 2027.
The key risks to this outlook are further worsening sentiment, a
return of deﬂation, more aggressive-than-expected rate cuts, or
tighter capital controls that keep domestic money locked up in the
CGB market.
Heavy bond issuance has been absorbed by
rising bank demand

9) How is the AI boom impacting China?
AI has become one of China’s more important growth engines,
supporting exports, manufacturing, investment and productivity. At
the same time, the heavy focus on the AI race means it is also a
major driver of the K-shaped divergence in China. It's likely to
continue to widen the gap between industries and companies.
China’s strong trade growth on both the export and import sides
were in large part tied to AI-related demand. Over 6,000 Chinese AI

ﬁrms across AI infrastructure, framework, and applications
contributed to heavy demand. We saw categories such as
automatic data processing machines, semiconductors, and general
computer & electronics with high double-digit and even triple-digit
export and import growth.
A similar pattern was observed in China’s manufacturing industry.
Technology-intensive manufacturing signiﬁcantly outperformed
the broader industrial in 2026, led by electronic equipment and

semiconductors. Chinese companies across a broad range of

### Page 18

industries are integrating AI into quality control, from precision
electronics manufacturing to chilli oil. AI is already playing a role in
China’s industrial upgrading process.
Hi-tech investment has been the lone bright spot in an otherwise
down year for investment, with hi-tech FAI up 4.9% YoY but
headline FAI down -7.2% YoY through 3Q26. Despite an equity
market selloﬀ as investors start to question valuations, the AI capex
cycle itself looks to have further room to run. Spending by major

Chinese tech companies has doubled from their 2022 levels, with
continued investment in computing capacity, data centres and AI
infrastructure. China’s grid investment data has been volatile but
has generally shown strong growth for the last few years as well.
The impact of AI on the labour market is a hot topic globally. In
China, aggregate employment data have yet to show a broad AI-
driven shock, but ﬁrm-level evidence already points to higher

output per worker and lower labour requirements in selected tasks.
The initial impact is therefore more likely to appear through slower
hiring and job redesign than immediate mass layoﬀs.
We think two factors could make this adjustment particularly
important for China in 2027:
AI adoption could move faster in China. China’s AI strategy
places greater emphasis on industrial deployment and
applications, while public trust and workplace usage are
relatively high compared to global peers. An ageing population

also gives ﬁrms a stronger incentive to automate in response
to further labour shortages. Faster AI adoption has shown
signs of supporting labour productivity growth. It has
accelerated for two straight years to reach 6.3% in 2025 after
dropping as low as 4.4% in 2023. But it could also accelerate
the displacement of substitutable roles and increase downward
occupational mobility.
Entry-level employment will face the greatest pressure. China
is expected to have 12.7 million university graduates in 2026,

480,000 more than in 2025, adding to an already competitive
youth labour market. At the same time, AI is increasingly able
Your privacy is important

### Page 19

to perform the information intensive and administrative work
which traditionally assigned to junior employees. Firms may
therefore reduce entry-level hiring without cutting their existing
workforce, narrowing the career ladder through which young
workers acquire skills and experience.
The government has rolled out policies to address the potential
impact of AI on employment, focusing on displacement risk
assessment, digital skills training, worker transitions and the

creation of new occupations. We expect these measures to
moderate, but not prevent, pressure on junior and routine white-
collar employment. The key issue for next year is whether new
service-sector and AI-complementary jobs can be created quickly
enough to absorb aﬀected workers.
Overall, we expect AI to support productivity growth and industrial
upgrading in 2027, but the gains will remain uneven. If commercial
returns disappoint the market’s high expectations, a reassessment

of the global AI cycle could weaken related demand. This could
leave China with stronger productive capacity but divergence across
industries and labour markets.
The other scenario worth considering is if we see a widespread AI
bust. Increasingly, market participants may increasingly shift their
focus to commercial returns of AI, where there is arguably still a lot
of froth in the markets. Given China’s heavy investment in the AI
race, and its prominent role in the outperforming areas of the

economy, a reversal of this trend would naturally have signiﬁcant
implications. We’ve already seen a signiﬁcant selloﬀ in equities,
which does not help the already soft conﬁdence. A moderate
pullback may not necessarily be bad news, as it could allow for
more balanced resource allocation elsewhere, but a crash would
pose larger risks to the growth outlook.
China's AI capex cycle accelerated sharply
after 2023

10) How is China faring in its Great
Transition?
Our key long-term call back when ﬁrst joining ING’s research
department in 2024 was that China was not in a Great Decline, but

Your privacy is important

Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 21

a Great Transition. At that time, discourse on China was arguably at
its most pessimistic, with many investors deeming China
uninvestable and amid worries over systemic risks. China’s
reopening after the pandemic fell short of expectations, and many
worried its outlook was doomed.
Almost three years later, China is in a signiﬁcantly diﬀerent place.
While a cloud of pessimism still hangs over the economy at large,
the narrative has become a lot more balanced. Within China’s

widening K-shaped trajectories, pockets of extremely strong growth
remain.
We outlined four main directions for China’s Great Transition:
Developing domestic demand and consumption as a growth
driver
Moving up the value-added ladder
Strengthening the green transition
Advancing tech and digitalisation
We’ve since seen progress across all four fronts, though the speed
has been uneven.
Developing domestic demand is arguably the most disappointing
of the four key transitions so far. Chinese households’ willingness

to save has hit a new high, and weak conﬁdence continues to
weigh on this eﬀort. Retail sales slowed to a post-pandemic low in
2026. Critics of China often cite weak domestic demand as a main
factor behind growing trade imbalances.
Policymakers have said all the right things. But for now, eﬀorts to
buoy domestic demand seem to have taken a backseat to
industrial modernisation and the tech race. We do, however, see
progress in the right direction. For example, trade-in programmes

helped front-load consumption, and policymakers have vowed to
“strengthen inclusive policies that reach consumers directly.”
China has already made great progress in moving up the value-
added ladder. The evolution of China’s industrial production and
exports shows its manufacturing backbone moving from the low
end toward the high end.

### Page 22

Exports show that the share of machinery and transport equipment
in total exports rose from 48.6% in 2023 to 56.0% in 3Q26.
Traditionally low-end miscellaneous manufacturing, including
clothing and footwear, has dropped from 22.8% in 2023 to 18.6%
in 3Q26.
This transition has major global implications, prompting discussion
of a so-called “China Shock 2.0” as the economy produces
increasingly high-end products. China's auto industry has rapidly
risen to global prominence, capitalising on the accelerating

worldwide adoption of electric vehicles.
China’s green transition has continued its great progress. China
has undergone a remarkable green transition over the past decade-
plus. Various indicators point to continued success in this green
transition in recent years. China’s PM 2.5 levels across 168 cities hit
a record low of 15 in July 2026. Renewable and nuclear power
generation as a percentage of total energy production rose from
21.5% at the end of 2023 to 26.2% at the end of 2025.

The green transition remains an important medium- and long-term
goal. As such, it will likely continue to outperform in terms of
growth. In 2025, President Xi pledged to lower emissions by 7-10%
by 2035 while “striving to do better”, building on 2020 comments
aiming to achieve peak carbon by 2030 and reach carbon
neutralisation by 2060.
Heavy investment continues in green industries. China is a leading
player in key sectors such as EVs, solar, and wind power generation.

This shift is clearly aﬀecting the global economy through trade and
investment.
China’s eﬀorts to advance technological development and
digitalisation have exceeded our expectations. Chinese ﬁrms have
proven to be competitive in the AI race. China has taken major
steps to improve self-reliance, including strengthening the domestic
semiconductor industry and developing the robotics industry.
Digitalisation of the economy continues to build on existing

strength. According to China’s National Data Administration, the

Monetary Policy Investment Inﬂation Imports GDP

core industries of China’s digital economy accounted for over
10.5% of total GDP in 2025.
There’s also a ﬁrm commitment to continue heavy investment in
research and development. China’s 15  Five-Year Plan committed
to setting an R&D expenditure growth target of at least 7% per year
through 2026-2030. R&D spending reached RMB 3.92tn in 2025,

equivalent to 2.8% of GDP.
In conclusion, China has made good but uneven progress in its key
economic transitions over the past few years. Strong progress in the
tech and green transitions, along with China’s aggressive move up
the value-added ladder, laid a foundation for future growth.
However, domestic demand must be strengthened to drive more
sustainable long-term growth. This is particularly true as we see
increasing pushback from trading partners about China’s growing

trade surplus. In China’s “dual circulation” strategy, the internal
circulation side must be strengthened in the coming months and
years.
The author would like to thank Sherry Chi for her contributions to this
article.

Document 006: 10月美国非农数据点评：新增非农大超预期，12月加息预期强化

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月美国非农数据点评：新增非农大超预期，12月加息预期强化.pdf
Report source: 交银国际证券

Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

此报告最后部分的分析师披露、商业关系披露和免责声明为报告的一部分，必须阅读。

交银国际研究 
宏观策略

2026年11月11日
全球宏观 
新增非农大超预期，12月加息预期强化——10月美国非农数据点评
10月非农数据显著高于市场预期。美国10月新增非农就业16.5万，市场
的预期增加7万。前两月的数据也都上修，9月数据首次修正上修3.1万人
到6.0万人，8月数据第二次修正上修至18.6万人，相对于初值上修了2.4
万人。
非季调的新增非农业显示就业增长强于去年。非季调非农就业10月增加
了102.3万人，去年10月仅增加62.6万人。去年10月季调后非农就业下
降14万人。我们在 上月数据点评中，也强调季调会引入噪音，虽然9月季
调后的新增非农偏弱，但是非季调今年的新增非农强于去年。
失业率小幅回落，劳动力市场整体仍不弱。失业率10月小幅下降到4.1%，
上月4.2%。从周期的角度看，目前的失业率水平仍然处于较低的水平，
离任何衰退时期的水平还较远。劳动参与率由61.8%进一步升至61.9%，
失业人口减少约4.1万人，因此失业率下降即来自分子的下降，也来自分
母的增长。
10月美国工资压力略有增加，尽管平均时薪同比增速小幅回落。10月美
国私营非农企业全体雇员平均时薪环比上涨0.4%，高于市场预期，同比
涨幅为3.1%，略低于9月修订后同比的3.2%，9月初值为3.0%。平均每周
工时维持在34.4小时。
10月高频数据显示失业仍处于较低水平。10月31日的当周初次申领失业
金人数为18.9万人，略低于上一周，近4周平均19.5万人，仍处于较低水
平。当前，美国就业市场处于“低招聘、低辞职”的状态，招聘率和辞职
率都不高。
12月美联储加息预期进一步增强。在强劲就业与工资数据影响下，市场
对12月美联储加息25个基点的概率由公布前约七成升至接近八成。我们
认为，12月继续加息仍是基准情形，其后的美联储政策路径仍需结合通
胀与就业质量进一步判断。
市场反应：美债利率小幅上行，美元指数上涨。数据公布当日，美股标普
收涨0.3%，纳斯达克上涨1.1%，道琼斯指数下跌0.5%。分行业看，标普
500行业指数中信息技术和通信服务当日涨幅靠前，前一日科技板块下跌
较多或是重要背景因素；而必需消费、金融、房地产下跌较多。美国国债
利率普遍小幅上行，10年期国债利率上升2个基点，1年期国债利率上升
5个基点，3个月国债利率上升2个基点。美元指数当日上涨0.7%。

评级定义
分析员个股评级定义 : 分析员行业评级定义：
买入：预期个股未来12个月的总回报高于相关行业。
中性：预期个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业一致。
沽出：预期个股未来12个月的总回报低于相关行业
无评级：对于个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业的比较，
分析员并无确信观点。
领先：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数具吸引力。
同步：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现与大盘标杆
指数一致。
落后：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数不具吸引力。

### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點：
• AI資料中心疑慮衝擊科技股: 民主黨在期中選舉獲勝, 多位民主黨州長重提AI建設禁令。市場擔心將影
響AI基建擴張, 參議員重提「超級AI禁令」更引發市場恐慌。
• AI熱潮面臨獲利考驗: AI算力需求仍強, 然市場進一步放大檢視「AI投資能創造多少收益」。AI基礎建
設受阻打擊AI供應鏈, 但資料中心回報率成長仍有利產業前景。
• 就業數據與美國經濟: 美國10月ISM製造業指數升至55.5創近4年新高, ADP新增就業8.6萬人, 優於市
場預期。經濟表現強勁, 通膨仍具黏性, Fed下一步仍傾向漸進升息。
2. 後續觀察重點：
• 10月CPI(11/10)、PPI(11/13)、輝達財報(11/18)。
策略思維
1. 股市-類股走勢分化: 選後政策預期變化, 加劇類股分化, 可提高非科技股比重, 透過持股多元分散, 降低投資
組合波動。科技股可逢低分批布局, 但宜控制持股比例。
2. 債市-Fed仍傾向升息, 殖利率高檔震盪: 經濟走強, 通膨具黏性, Fed仍有升息壓力, 資料中心建設受阻降低

資金及舉債需求, 10年債殖利率回落, 仍維持震盪格局。
3. 匯市-美元高檔震盪: 美元指數近期隨美債殖利率回落, 但受升息預期影響而維持高檔震盪格局。

民主黨在參眾兩院皆取得過半席次 美股年末表現
資料來源: 270towin.com, 截至2026/11/4。 註: 統計1986年以來資料。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。
美國｜期中選舉結果出爐, 民主黨在參眾兩院皆取得過半席次
• 美國府會衝突與「否決先生」: 川普失去國會支持, 剩餘任期出現跛腳危機, 目前白宮已研擬「無國會施政」劇本,
可能透過行政命令與關稅延續政策, 川普更宣稱將動用總統否決權來阻擋不認同的法案。
• 民主黨的制衡與調查: 民主黨宣稱將透過國會調查川普家族與政府機構, 更可能全面封殺川普的司法與高層人事任
命。金融市場並不擔心分裂國會帶來的政治僵局, 這將會削弱川普極端政策或激進財政擴張的可能性, 反而降低市
場不確定因素。金融市場更加關注的是, 選舉結果將主導科技、醫療、能源等核心產業政策的重分配。

Cathay’s View
美國AI資料中心禁令
資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/6。
• 隨著美國期中選舉出爐, 民主黨多位勝選候選人已經在當選演說中, 再度將AI、資料中心及大型科技公司的監管問
題搬上檯面, 包括俄亥俄州、維吉尼亞州議員及伊利諾州州長, 他們強調從資料中心到AI安全, 都不能交由少數科
技巨頭決定。
• 整體而言, 川普政府加速發展AI的方向不變, 但地方政府對資料中心的土地、電力及環境審查可能更加嚴格, 根據
UBS先前預估, 2028年的資料中心擴建可能有30%受到衝擊, 高盛預估有45%的影響。
資料中心｜資料中心建置將受到一定的衝擊
州長／德州Gina
Hinojosa(政見)
1. 暫緩新建資料中心: 暫停新資料中心興建及核准, 直到州議會制定完善法規, 保障居民參與
地方開發決策的權利。
2. 取消企業補貼、落實成本自負：要求業者自行承擔電力、用水及電網建設成本, 取消大型
企業租稅優惠, 避免成本轉嫁給一般民眾。

州長／伊利諾州JB
Pritzker(感言) 強調伊利諾州已制定全美最嚴格的AI監管規範, 科技富豪不能凌駕法律。
州長／賓州Josh
Shapiro(感言) 強調不能讓大型科技公司制定影響未來的規則, 地方社區應有最終決定權。
參議員當選人／俄亥俄州
Sherrod Brown(感言) 「I do not work for data centers.」（我不是為資料中心業者工作的)。
參議員當選人／南卡羅來
納州Annie Andrews(感言)社區決策應由當地居民決定, 而非華府的億萬富豪或資料中心業者。
參議員／維吉尼亞州Mark
Warner(感言)
AI雖有創新與創造產業的機會, 但也有負面影響, 從資料中心到AI安全, 都不能交由少數科技
巨頭決定。

Cathay’s View
2028年的資本支出可能受波及
註: 5大CSP業者指谷歌、微軟、Meta、亞馬遜及甲骨文。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/3。
• 在AI需求持續強勁之際 , 大型雲端服務供應商擴大資本支出已成市場共識 , 市場預估2026 年整體資本支出將達
8,000億美元, 2027年更有望正式突破1兆美元。然而, 隨著AI監管趨嚴, 加上部分州政府開始考慮暫緩資料中心興
建, 未來AI基礎建設的擴張速度可能受到影響。
• 短期而言, 預期2027年CSP資本支出仍具一定延續性, 但若地方政府陸續提高建置門檻, 2028年投資計畫可能面臨
較明顯的延遲或調整。後續應持續關注大型CSP的資料中心布局, 是否轉往政策較友善的州別, 或進一步擴大海外
投資, 這將是影響未來AI資本支出規模與成長速度的重要變數。

GPU出租價格仍持續上漲, 降低折舊風險 雲端營收持續高成長
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。 註: 谷歌、微軟、亞馬遜雲端營收合計。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/4。
• 隨資料中心積極擴張資本支出, 市場擔憂未來AI投資與折舊成本的顯著增加, 將高於未來營收增速, AI投資回報率
下降。惟算力供給吃緊, 新舊GPU租金都持續上漲, 2022年量產的H100晶片, 折舊成本已接近攤平, 未來的租金收
入反而都將快速提升資料中心的投資回報率。
• 若由3大CSP業者雲端業務營收來觀察, 2026Q3合計營收已突破1000億美元, 2028年彭博預估更高達8932億美元,
若由於AI資料中心基建受阻而影響算力供給, 資料中心的投資回報率提高, 大型CSP仍是最大受惠族群。

Anthropic Q3營收168億美元, 已成軟體巨擎 Meta主業廣告營收受惠AI成長加速
資料來源: Anthropic預估, 預估期間為2026Q3, 截至2026/10/26。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/4。
科技｜AI應用擴大, 軟體及終端收入明顯增加
• AI應用持續由基礎建設向軟體與終端服務擴散, 商業化速度明顯加快。Anthropic第三季營收達168億美元, 顯示生
成式AI需求已由模型訓練延伸至企業應用, 軟體業者營收規模快速放大, AI產業成長動能更趨全面。
• 終端應用亦開始展現AI帶來的實質效益, Meta核心廣告業務受惠AI強化推薦、廣告投放與轉換效率, 營收成長進一
步加速。隨AI投資逐步轉化為軟體訂閱、廣告及服務收入, 有助市場焦點由資本支出轉向變現能力。

註: 2-4月受就業與消費數據走弱影響、7-9月受就業及製造業數據降溫拖累, 經濟驚奇
指數回落; 惟油價與通膨預期影響, 美債殖利率維持高檔。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/2。
• Fed官員延續鷹派: 除10月支持升息的兩名官員以外, 近期多位官員亦重申持續升息方向, 成員涵蓋今年及明年具
投票權的委員。Fed態度暗示的漸進式升息立場大致與期貨市場預估一致, 料未來1年仍具2~3碼升息空間。
• 經濟數據強勁: 美國第三季GDP、10月ISM、9月JOLTS等數據均優於市場預期, 以及PCE增速持續走高, 經濟驚奇
指數的反彈, 恐緩和近期科技股賣壓 、高利吸引力推動的債市買盤效應 。雖今年經濟驚奇指數與 10年債利率走勢

時有背離, 但油價高檔、通膨預期擔憂仍使利率走勢不易呈現趨勢性下行。
• 展望後市, 1)期中選舉後的中東局勢仍有高度地緣不確定性, 2)季度再融資計畫反映公債供給壓力, 3)預估2027年
AI信用債淨發行規模 恐持續擴張。在通膨、債務供給與資金需求 三重因素支撐下, 高利率中樞環境將為市場常態。
債市｜聯準會官員延續鷹派、數據反映經濟韌性,  利率大幅下行空間受限

融資需求居高不下, 2027Q1升至8000億美元 新增發債需求更偏短天期
註: 採12個月滾動加總(12M rolling sum)淨發行, 近一年淨發行約2兆美元。
資料來源: Bloomberg, Sifma, 截至2026/9/30。
財政部｜美國2027年Q1借款需求攀升, 短債供給占比將逐步升高
註: 單位為億美元。
資料來源: 美國財政部預估。
• 最新季度借款預估顯示, Q3實際淨發債規模7420億美元, 大致符合8月預估值(7390億美元)。季末TGA餘額9840億
美元亦高於目標9500億美元, 導致Q4淨發債需求較前次預估下修100億美元; 惟考量期初現金餘額影響, 借款需求
實際較前次預估增加240億美元。2027年第一季借款規模預估8000億美元, 財政融資需求仍維持高檔。
• 財政部維持「Bills優先、Coupon穩定」的發債策略, 短期新增融資需求主要由國庫券吸收, 以避免中長天期公債
供給進一步增加。此舉有助控制發債成本, 但也意味市場對短端資金需求的依賴度持續提升; 目前Bills占未償還市

場性公債比重約22%(截至9/30), 高於TBAC(財政部借款諮詢委員會)建議的20%水準, 未來若借貸需求擴大, 市場
對流動性事件的尾端風險擔憂及再融資壓力的關注度將進一步提升。

第三季GDP年化季增3.4% 核心PCE年增率升高
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q3。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 美國第三季GDP年化季增3.4%, 增速創一年來新高, 並高於市場預期3.2%及Q2的2.2%。除了個人消費強勁, 民間
投資成長顯著: 在AI投資熱潮帶動下, 設備支出對GDP貢獻創2011年以來新高, 為支撐經濟成長強勁的兩大因素。
• 9月PCE與核心PCE分別年增3.6%及3.1%。以核心通膨觀察, 除了房租價格走高之外, 扣除住房的服務指標「超級
核心」亦同步反彈: 能源價格走升帶動運輸服務, 醫療保健、金融保險增速降幅有限, 核心通膨仍具黏性。
• 美國經濟活動持續擴張, 消費與企業投資表現同步穩健, 展現經濟韌性; 與此同時, 通膨增速居高不下, 均將支持聯
準會維持偏緊縮的貨幣政策立場, 隱含未來進一步升息空間。

10月ADP新增8.6萬與前月持平, 服務業仍為主要支撐 JOLTS反映就業信心改善
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 10月ADP新增就業8.6萬人, 優於市場預期(7.5萬人)。主要由服務業帶動 (貿易/運輸/公用事業+3.9萬、教育/醫療
+2.8萬、休閒餐旅+1.9萬), 但專業與商業服務(-2.7萬)及商品製造相關行業仍顯疲弱。
• 9月JOLTS職缺數+33.3萬至752.3萬, 職缺/失業比同步上升; 從新增比例觀察, 政府部門+10.4萬職缺(約1/3), 勞動
需求屬溫和回穩, 非私部門全面性爆發。細項方面, 自主離職數(轉職信心)反彈、裁員率減少, 均為正向就業訊號。
• 雖美國勞動市場自疫情後的高峰放緩, 但目前低裁員率 、穩定就業成長仍反映就業韌性。隨市場對勞動需求走弱
的擔憂淡化, Fed短期內亦無須過度關注就業下行風險, 政策焦點仍將著重在通膨進展。

英國物價與央行利率 MPC委員投票傾向
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/5。
英國BoE｜英央政策偏鷹調整, 市場上修升息次數
• 英國9月CPI年增率由8月3.1%升至3.5%, 能源與運輸價格仍是主要推手, 服務業通膨升至3.7%, 顯示物價壓力已不
再侷限於能源項目, 而有逐步擴散至內需部門的跡象。雖然失業率維持4.7%, 且職缺數持續下降, 但私人部門薪資
增速回升至3.0%, 10月綜合PMI升至53.1, 代表現行利率水準對需求抑制效果可能不如預期。
• 因此, 11月MPC投票結果由先前 6:3支持按兵不動 , 轉變為全面升息 。同步上修 2026、27年經濟成長至 1.6%和
1.3%。大幅上調通膨預期 , 預估將在2027年年中攀升至5%左右(不利情境下甚至可能升至 6.2%)。導致今年年底

升息機率提高至 50%附近, 2027年2月升息一次的機率幾乎底定 , 政策基調已由 「觀察能源衝擊是否短暫 」轉向
「預防通膨預期失控」。

英國公共部門淨借款占GDP比重預測 長天期利率
資料來源: OBR預估, 截至2026/10。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/3。
英國預算｜財政赤字上修與公債壓力仍在
• 英國最新秋季預算案延續 「財政紀律」主軸, 聚焦於降低家庭生活成本與支持企業投資 。主要措施包括向約2000
萬戶家庭提供一次性 £100能源補貼、將家用電力增值稅降至 0%的措施延長至2027年7月, 以及延長每公升5便士
的燃油稅減免與燃油稅凍結政策 。同時擴大服務業減免範圍 、提高酒吧與音樂場館商業稅減免 , 並將個人營業收
入免稅額由1000提高至3000英鎊, 銀行附加稅自3%提高至6%, 並加強稅務稽核以增加收入來源。

• 儘管新增支出相對克制, 英國財政前景仍較3月預算案略為惡化。預測顯示未來數年財政整頓速度放慢 , 對市場而
言, 這代表未來英國公債(Gilts)供給將維持高檔, 長端殖利率易保持在較高水準。

RBA調低成長率, 調高失業率與通膨預期 市場預期澳洲升息循環將更快見頂
澳洲｜澳洲央行如預期升息一碼, 但升息循環告終可能性大幅提高
• 繼9月按兵不動後 , 澳洲央行11月利率決議如市場預期升息一碼至 4.85%, 但同時出現3票反對, 同時聲明上刪除
「必要時進一步升息」的字眼, 市場解讀為「鷹派升息、鴿派暗示」, 本次升息循環可能接近終點。
• 總裁Bullock表示, 他們對成長、房市、勞動市場的下行風險權重高於通膨上行風險, 並主張考量已累積的升息與政
策時滯, 應等待更多資訊 。顯示RBA在打擊通膨的同時, 仍不得不向疲軟的經濟妥協 。考量升息循環已接近終點 ,
對澳幣的看法轉趨中立, 但對於澳債仍正向看待。

美元高檔徘徊, 新台幣壓力未散 外資賣壓沉重, 為新台幣疲弱主因
資料來源: Bloomberg,截至2026/11/4。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。
• 10月外資賣超9943億元, 創下台股史上最高單月賣超紀錄, 11月賣超3307億元, 累計賣超逾1.3兆元, 為新台幣近期
急貶主因。
• 近期美台股市反覆震盪, 帶動外資在台股的獲利了結賣壓, 成為新台幣近期走弱主因。央行雖有進場干預, 但均以
維持當日匯率穩定為主, 未干預新台幣短期方向。後續若股市打底, 外資賣壓有望減輕。

費半跌破7月低點, 健護指數衝高 美債殖利率回落, 地產指數反彈
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。
美股｜選後政策預期變化, 加劇類股走勢分化
• 民主黨拿下參、眾兩院過半席次, 並在地方選舉大勝, 政策預期變化加劇美股類股分化。資料中心禁建風潮恐拉長
申請審核流程, 遞延AI伺服器訂單, 半導體股持續修正, 費半指數跌破7月低點; 相較之下, 市場預期川普政府激進的
醫療削減政策將受到制衡, 使得健護產業成為選後主要的政策受惠者, 資金明顯轉進。
• 另一方面, 若資料中心建設放緩, 對資金及發債的需求降低, 美債殖利率應聲回落, 緩解利率敏感型產業的評價壓力,
地產指數明顯反彈。市場資金由高估值科技股轉向政策及利率受惠族群, 短期類股輪動加快, 美股多元配置重要性
進一步提升。

AI需求強勁, 帶動AMD資料中心翻倍成長 AMD毛利率續揚, 顯示需求依舊緊俏
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/4。
• AMD第三季營收達133.8億美元, 年增45%, 高於市場預期, 非GAAP EPS達2.09美元, 年增74%, 同樣優於預期。
其中, 資料中心部門營收達85.5億美元, 年增97%, 創下歷史新高。此外, 毛利率達56%, 年增2個百分點, 營業利益
率則提升至29%, 反映AI業務規模擴大正帶動獲利能力改善。
• 展望方面, AMD對第四季及2027年營運前景展現高度信心。公司預估第四季營收可達170億美元, 年增約66%, 不
僅高於市場預期, 也意味營收成長動能將進一步加速。執行長蘇姿丰亦看好2027年營收成長可望超過80%, 主要來
自AI GPU出貨擴大, 以及伺服器CPU需求持續成長。不過, 遊戲業務營收年減40%, 顯示非AI市場仍存在壓力。

外資在韓股拋售力道仍大 韓股與韓元近期出現罕見的股匯反向
韓股｜近期外資拋售力道不減, 但韓股與韓元出現罕見股匯反向
• 外資對韓股的拋售未歇, 且此次企業回購結束, 本土法人與散戶買盤力道有限, 加上近期市場對於AI類股的疑慮加
深。基本面敘事改變與籌碼壓力下使韓股持續弱勢震盪。
• 另一方面, 韓股與韓元出現罕見的股匯反向, 在大漲/大跌的波動日尤其明顯, 與台股常見的外資賣、股市跌、台幣
貶的典型路徑相反。研判原因為(1)今年國際化改革後, 外資可直接於在岸市場交易韓元, 對本土匯市影響更直接。
(2)目前韓股外資的邊際流量以短線基金的快錢為主, 買/賣股同時搭配匯率避險/解除避險。整體而言, 韓股能否重
拾漲勢仍端看市場何時對AI產業的疑慮緩解。

五中全會要點 官方救市工具
中國｜著眼換屆, 五中全會先為未來一年定調; 人行等金融部門則聯手公布穩市政策
• 中共五中全會聚焦「持之以恒推進全面從嚴治黨」, 強調黨性修養、從嚴治吏、正風肅紀與反腐敗常態化, 並完成
重要人事調整與紀律確認。政治安全與權力集中置於優先位置, 這使得政策環境更偏向可控, 而非積極擴張。在考
核與問責壓力下, 官員避險心態可能延續, 進而影響經濟政策的執行力度與市場預期的修復速度。
• 與此同時, 人行會同國家金融監督管理總局、中國證監會三部門聯手開會, 重點有二: 1)將股票回購再貸款利率下
調一碼至1%, 以及2)第三批證券基金保險互換便利(Securities, Funds and Insurance companies Swap Facility,
SFISF)互換便利達1000億元申請(近前兩次合計), 以引導更多資金進入股市, 回應市場對「穩市」的期待, 有助提
振信心。
資料來源: Wind, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/3。 資料來源: Wind, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/3。

大盤企業獲利增長仍舊疲弱 A股指數與評價走勢
中國｜央媽祭出政策嘗試托底, 但大規模增量政策暫且無望, 加上國際氣氛影響, 指數需時間築底
• 在五中全會的政策指導下, 政治穩定先於經濟發展, 短期內難見大規模增量政策。考量現階段, 社會融資的年增速
仍偏緩慢, 顯示實體經濟融資需求與信用擴張動能尚未明顯回升。大盤企業整體獲利年增速依舊偏弱, 獲利修復基
礎不夠牢固。這進一步限制了市場上行動能, 加上近期國際股市氣氛不佳, 也使得估值修復難以一蹴而就。
• 考量數據與治理取向, 1)短線行情偏淡、指數需時間築底, 因為大規模刺激期待落空, 加上國際氣氛讓AI硬體股跟
跌, 長期均線呈現空頭排列下, 大盤轉為防禦與避險主導; 2)中長線在黨建先於發展的邏輯下, 資金還是只能流向國
企色彩高的類股, 加以人行將股票回購再貸款利率再度下調, 這應能為具有股息誘因的金融股形成支撐, 是大盤守

勢下, 非科技股的另一選項。
資料來源: Wind, 截至2026/11/3。 資料來源: Wind, 截至2026/11/3。

AI 估值疑慮引發波動，惟股市仍有多頭可期
2026 年 7 月
全球主要股市 6 月 （截至24 日） 報酬跌多漲少，與5 月新興亞洲及 AI 半導體題材主導下的多頭
格局形成鮮明對比，主要因為市場同時承受美伊衝突升溫、 Fed 新主席華許上任後鷹派轉向，以及
AI 類股估值偏高引發的修正壓力，風險資產動能熄火。以美元計價6 月表現觀察，MSCI 中國與拉丁
美洲指數分別下跌 5.9%及 4.5%，表現最差。中國主因房市持續疲弱與內需復甦不均，並受其對中東
原油進口的高度依賴拖累 ， 拉丁美洲則受前波累積漲幅較大後的獲利了結與美元期間走強雙重壓抑。

美股因 AI 估值疑慮再起而拉回，拖累MSCI 新興市場與新興亞洲分別下跌 1.3%。惟有 MSCI 印度逆
勢上漲 1.9%，主要受惠 6 月下旬國際油價回落帶動。整體而言，6 月股市回檔較像是高基期後的短
線修正，並未改變今年資金偏好新興亞洲、日本與科技成長市場的方向。
6 月（截至 24 日）全球債市呈現「利率類承壓、信用類抗跌」的結構性分歧。全球主權債券單
月下跌 1.03%，跌幅居首，主因華許於 6 月會議釋出鷹派訊號，18 位官員中有 9 位於點陣圖計入今
年內至少一次升息，使美國 10 年期公債殖利率一度升至 4.5%，存續期間較長之利率類債券遭逢重
估。全球投資級公司債下跌 0.67%，跌幅次之，亦受存續期間拖累。新興當地債小跌 0.12%，反映
因美元指數回升使來自匯率的貢獻由正轉負。信用類債券則表現相對 較佳，新興美元債券單月上漲
0.53%、亞洲高收益債上漲 0.49%領先，主要受惠信用利差續處歷史低檔、企業基本面強健且違約率
仍偏低、到期牆延後且供給以再融資為主。

近期股市雖因 Fed 升息疑慮及 AI 類股估值過高而修正，但美光財報及蘋果商品漲價均印證 AI
產品供需緊俏且企業資本支出依然強勁 。 市場近期以類股輪動方式進行修正 ， 顯示投資人對於整體股
市信心仍在。在投資策略上，短期不宜追逐高估值個股，但可於震盪時分批布局 AI 基礎建設、半導
體供應鏈及台日股市。
債市方面，當前美債提供高於過往均值的高殖利率，將可相當程度抵銷殖利率續揚的風險。然因
投資人對 Fed 後續貨幣政策存有疑慮，或導致殖利率曲線短端受升息預期而有較大波動；長端又有
期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供給增加等風險。因此，建議債券佈局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國
公債及投等債，不僅能提供相對具吸引力的息收，若未來通膨預期放緩，在資本利得亦有表現機會。

美國股市：企業盈餘亮點集中 AI，股市評價尚屬合理，經濟成長趨緩但韌性猶存
美股第一季財報顯示，標普 500 成分股獲利有八成企業超越市場預期，整體每股盈餘同比增長
26.2%，創下 2021 年以來新高，企業盈餘上修動能強勁。隨運算需求從大模型訓練轉向推論，市場
資金快速轉向記憶體、光通訊、ASIC、CPU 與通用型伺服器供應鏈，引領板塊快速輪動。接下來第
二季 AI 產業仍將帶動資訊業獲利高增長，而能源、原物料產業第二季財報仍有不錯表現，但消費、
金融、醫療等，甚至通訊服務產業都有成長放緩跡象。
美國關稅談判正轉向以「301 法案」推進，目前針對「強迫勞動貨品進口禁令」，美國貿易代表

署（USTR） 已建議對全球60 個經濟體加徵 10%與 12.5%的額外關稅 。 預料下半年陸續將有更多301
調查結果公布，這將為川普關稅談判提供法源依據，迫使各國供應鏈加速赴美設廠，為美國製造業注
入投資動能，故對美國整體經濟表現無須看淡。
伴隨企業獲利預期上修 ， 標普500 指數前瞻本益比目前仍在 21.45 倍水準 ， 且通訊服務僅在20.9
倍、資訊科技僅在 24.8 倍，估值水平依舊合理。當前市場質疑重點在於AI 產業吸引大量資金投入資
本支出，故縮減企業發放現金股利與股票回購的能力，然而考量下半年仍有期中選舉行情可期，且
AI 產業變革快速，新題材層出不窮，操作上維持分批布局的建議。
美國經濟在 2026 年表現顯示成長趨緩但韌性猶存，2026 年第一季 GDP 成長率為 2.6%，表現
仍優於歐元區、日本等其他市場。失業率維持在低檔，5 月非農新增就業仍有 17.2 萬人，失業率持
平於 4.3%。5 月 CPI 年增率升至 4.3%， 核心CPI 年增率加速到 2.9%， 能源價格仍為推升通膨主因；

同月份 PPI 與核心 PPI 分別來到 6.5%與 4.9%， 顯示上游成本壓力正持續向下游轉嫁。勞動市場結構
性分歧，金融就業人數下滑，然而資料中心、半導體與電力等基礎建設投資，拉動非住宅營建業與製
造業僱傭需求，支撐整體就業數據，消費端方面，5 月美國零售銷售月增率回升至 0.9%，其中加油
站銷售與無店鋪零售商銷售(電商)表現強勁，前月走弱的汽車及零部件銷售逆勢反彈，顯示在K 型經
濟支撐下，高收入群體與特定消費仍表現穩健。
預料新任主席華許在確立聯準會改革方案前不急於升息，但目前利率仍處歷史高位，對房市、汽
車和消費造成壓抑 。 觀察密西根大學消費者信心指數在第二季來到有紀錄以來最低 ， 且美國新屋開工
下降，房市持續低迷，美國經濟仍需消化高利率與通膨帶來的負面效應。

歐洲股市：歐洲央行態度轉鷹，靜待美伊停火與關稅明朗化
歐洲央行在 6 月會議升息 1 碼，並持續警告中東地緣政治風險可能為通膨前景增添不確定性，因
此將今年通膨預期上調至 3.0%（前值 2.6%） ， 預計要到明年下半年才有機會回到 2% 的政策目標 。
不過，與 2022 年通膨高峰時期不同，目前歐洲就業市場並未出現明顯過熱，薪資壓力也未持續升
溫，因此我們認為本次升息後，歐洲央行更可能轉向觀望，利率期貨則顯示，市場仍預期年底前有再
升息 1 碼的可能性。
歐洲央行本次會議將邊際貸款利率、主要再融資利率及存款機制利率上調 1 碼至 2.65% 、

2.4% 與 2.25% ，會議中所有委員均同意升息。勞動力需求進一步降溫，薪資成長預計將在一年內
放緩，目前沒有看到與工資相關的二次效應，本次會議未討論中性利率和中性區間，如果不升息，通
膨因此失控 ， 重新控制將更加困難 。GDP 成長預估來看 ，2026 年爲 0.8%，2027 年爲 1.2%，2028
年爲 1.5% 。通膨方面，2026 年爲 3%，2027 年爲 2.3%，2028 年爲 2.0% 。核心通膨來看，
2026 年爲 2.5%，2027 年爲 2.5%，2028 年爲 2.2% 。
歐洲經濟成長動能放緩，但製造業維持擴張態勢，雖然歐洲央行將 2026、2027 年經濟成長率預
估值下調，自 0.9%、1.3%調整至 0.8%、1.2%。然 6 月歐元區綜合 PMI 自 48.5 回升至 49.5，顯示
景氣萎縮幅度正逐步收窄，製造業活動仍維持在擴張區間，同月服務業 PMI 雖僅 48.9，但也已釋出
跌勢趨緩訊號。
ECB 升息 1 碼對抗通膨風險，後續升息路徑分歧，受美伊衝突引發的油價影響，歐洲通膨壓力再

度上升，5 月歐元區 CPI 年增率已達 3.2%，為防範通膨失控，歐洲央行在 6 月利率決策會議將存款
利率從 2.0%上調至 2.25%。目前市場對歐洲央行後續的升息路徑看法顯著分歧，鷹派陣營認為 6 月
升息 1 碼後、第三季會再升 1 碼，並維持利率水平至 2027 年底；鴿派觀點認為隨油價回跌，短期內
再升息必要性降低，故後續仍將觀察各國通膨轉嫁能力。
待美伊戰事落幕、美歐關稅底定，歐股可望啟動估值修復，展望後市，若美伊停火協議落實、引
領國際油價回吐先前漲幅，將成為扭轉歐股當前弱勢、並吸引國際資金回流的催化劑。此外，美國改
採以 301 條款課徵關稅，對歐洲出口實質影響仍須觀察，惟預料美歐盟友關係穩固，終將透過談判
達成雙邊協議。目前泛歐 600 本益比以 2026 年獲利僅 15.6 倍。待短期不確定性因素陸續消退，市
場情緒可望改善，有利啟動估值修復行情。

日本市場：成長動能仰賴出口，政府補貼支撐景氣表現，股市屢創新高
GDP 動能放緩，受惠族群集中於AI，日本 2026 年第一季 GDP 年增率僅 0.4%，創下 2024 年走
出經濟衰退以來最疲弱表現，市場已將全年度 GDP 成長預估下調至 0.6%。然而 5 月出口年增來到
17%，創 2022 年 11 月以來最快增速，其中半導體相關出口年增逾六成、反映全球資料中心需求全
面爆發，推升記憶體晶片與電子零組件價格走揚，故受惠族群集中，下半年成長動能仍高度仰賴 AI
產業。
財政補貼壓低通膨 ， 油價回跌降低財政負擔，近期 ，首相高市早苗推行 「責任的積極財政」 政策，

透過實施電費、瓦斯補貼，並將全國汽油價格維持在每公升約170 日圓水平。未來隨補貼措施退場，
預期物價將出現階段性反彈。日本央行已於利率決策會議把本年度核心通膨預估上調至 2.8%。然而
在利率走高背景下，日本政府財政結構惡化，根據2026 財年政府預算規劃，國債利息支出將達總預
算的 10.65%，明顯高於前一財年的 9.13%。所幸美伊戰事有降溫跡象，預期日本政府將持續縮減刺
激方案規模。
日銀升息 1 碼，美日利差未收斂壓抑日圓表現，且日本央行於 6 月會議決定升息 1 碼至 1.0%，
主要考量國內物價上行風險加劇、薪資維持強勁成長動能，且經濟下行風險受控。市場普遍預期下次
升息落在 2026 年第四季。然而高市若為緩解財政壓力，可能干預日銀升息路徑。鑑於美日利差未出
現明顯收斂 ， 即使日本官方進場干預 ， 對日圓匯率的支撐效果恐怕相對有限 ， 日圓匯率預估於155-163
區間震盪。
日經指數近期屢創新高，6 月 22 日史上首度突破 72,000 點、連七個交易日刷新紀錄。上漲動能

來自日圓貶至 161 附近，顯著優化出口企業的盈餘表現，同時「實體 AI」投資計畫引爆半導體與 AI
類股行情 。 就2026 年以來 ， 領漲標的涵蓋東京威力科創 、 村田製作所 、 鎧俠 、 軟銀、 三菱UFJ 金融、
愛德萬測試、松下控股、三井住友金融、瑞穗金融、藤倉等。上漲族群主要為 AI 供應鏈以及受惠於
升息的金融族群，但即使過去一年日股飆升，目前以 2026 年獲利，本益比仍在 24 倍合理位置。
波克夏深化日本投資佈局，外資買盤同步加碼，波克夏對日本資產配置持續增加。自 2020 年對
伊藤忠、丸紅、三菱商事、三井物產、住友商事等五家各以略高於 5%持股起步，至 2026 年 5 月對
各家持股均已突破 10%， 顯示加碼日本仍是其核心策略 。 與此同時 ， 外資對日股買盤持續湧入 ，2026
年迄今(截至 6 月 20 日為止)外資買超日股的累計金額擴大至 10.2 兆日圓 ， 反映國際資本對日股後勢
依舊樂觀。

台灣股市：AI 需求支撐製造業 PMI 高檔擴張，暑假旺季與股市推升服務動能
當前臺灣經濟正處於生產擴張的黃金期。最新公布的總體經濟數據與半導體龍頭營收呈現全面共
振，印證全球 AI 資本支出（CapEx）並非流於故事題材，而是實質轉化為台廠的訂單與獲利。儘管
全球金融市場短期受到地緣政治干擾與美債殖利率波動影響 ， 引發台股在高檔震盪 ， 但主計總處大幅
上修 2026 年 GDP 預測至 9.64%，配合製造業 PMI 續擴張至 60.7%，顯示基本面強勁。我們認
為，台股高檔的回檔屬於健康的獲利了結與籌碼沉澱，長線多頭架構未變。建議採取拉回布局策略，

聚焦 AI 實體基礎設施與邊緣運算（Edge AI）雙主線。
國發會公布之 6 月製造業 PMI 報 60.7， 連續第 6 個月維持在 60.0%以上的擴張速度 ， 六大產業
中，僅食品暨紡織產業與交通工具回報緊縮。細項中，新增訂單與生產分別上揚至 60.4（前 59.6）
與 58.7（前 57.9） ，同時原物料存貨和客戶存貨分別回跌至 61.9（前 65.7）和 49.4（前 50.5），
顯示在 AI 伺服器、半導體與高階製造的強勁拉貨下，庫存水位仍屬可控。
5 月海關出口達 784.8 億美元（年增 51.7%） ，寫下連 31 紅紀錄。其中，資通訊產品出口年
增 75.2%，電子零組件出口創歷史新高。同時，5 月進口額創下 605.7 億美元歷史新高（年增
54.9%） ，核心物資多為半導體資本設備，依進口領先出口 1 至 2 季之歷史慣例，預示下半年出貨
動能將再創高峰。台積電 5 月合併營收達 4,169 億 7,500 萬元（年增 30.1%） 。先進製程與高階
封裝產能持續滿載，且透過定價調整順利維持毛利率在高檔，讓獲利能見度直接延伸至後續數年。

主計總處正式將 2026 全年經濟成長率（GDP）預測大幅上修 1.93 個百分點至 9.64%，創下
16 年來新高紀錄。第一季 GDP 初估更達 14.55%，創 48 年來單季新高。 總經結構分析顯示，
這波榮景是由「出口拉動投資、投資進而促進消費」的良性循環。全球主要雲端服務供應商（CSP）
調高資本支出，其新一代伺服器建置「必須向台灣採購」 。商品出口實質成長預估將帶動全年商品出
口總值達 8,945 億美元，其年增率創 50 年來最高水準，構成台灣 GDP 增長的關鍵引擎。
儘管台股指數在高檔震盪加劇，但在 9.64% 的 GDP 強勁基本面保護下，下檔支撐極度紮實。
隨著 COMPUTEX 後終端裝置陸續開出 ， 消費性電子將迎來升級週期 ， 可望讓下半年經濟加速升溫。
我們持續看多台灣股市。

中國股市：製造業需求回溫，然動能僅集中於高新技術產業與出口端
中國 6 月製造業需求回溫，動能集中新產業與出口端，製造業 PMI 為 50.3（前 50.0） ，較前
月回升，並連續四個月維持在擴張區間。主要動能來自新出口訂單回升至 50.1（前 48.6） ，以及新
訂單升至 51.2（前 49.9） ，皆較前月回升至擴張區間，顯示需求端及出口端表現亦有所回溫。生產
經營活動預期上升至 54.3（前 53.9） ，製造業前景保有一定韌性，細項觀察，6 月生產指數 51.4
（前 51.2） ，持續維持在擴張區間，本月在新訂單回升、庫存指數回落至 47.7（前 49.3）之下，新

訂單減庫存擴大至 3.5（前 0.6） 。 另外受到美伊衝突緩解影響 ， 主要原材料價格大幅回落至 54.2（前
60.5） ，外部壓力緩解。
產業方面，新舊動能延續上月分化格局。高技術製造業 PMI 升至 53.5（前 52.9） ，裝備製造業
升至 52.5（前 52.1） ，上升趨勢持續，相較之下，傳統高耗能產業 47.1（前 47.1） ，建築業商業活
動 49.0（前 48.8） ，仍維持在較弱格局。整體來看，美伊衝突緩解有效帶動中國製造廠商轉趨樂觀，
但多數仍集中在新產業及出口端，後續仍待觀察新產業能否有效帶動內需動能回升。
然而，內需數據尚未見回溫：中國第二季以來的消費、固定投資數據下滑，房地產未見起色。工
業生產年增長 4.5%，表現最為堅挺，主要受到政策性資本開支與科技硬需求的支撐。零售銷售5 月
單月年增率轉負， 即便有五一黃金周的短暫拉動，但節後服務消費迅速墜落顯示出居民消費依然保
守。固定資產投資年比下降 4.1% 。 這是過往作為壓艙石的投資首次跌破 4% 防線。民間固定資

產投資年比更劇烈下滑 7.1%。
整體來說，中國政府大規模刺激的可能性偏低，主要政策料是調降 LPR 或結構性定向降準。其
政策核心在於為中下游民營中小企業減輕債務壓力。現行溫和的企業設備更新與家電 「以舊換新」 政
策邊際效益遞減。為遏制消費滑入通縮螺旋，中央財政下半年將擴大赤字，直接補貼居民端，如發放
消費券、加大育兒養老津貼等等。中國總體經濟正處於重組期，缺乏向上突破的動能，但向下有國家
政策工具與新興硬科技的實質托底 。 房地產全面反轉或大眾消費復甦的機率偏低 。 雖政府政策扶植的
新科技產業仍有機會，但整體看法仍偏向保守。

印度股市：外部供給衝擊與內部基本面的對抗，看法中性
全球供應鏈受到中東戰事的實質干擾 ， 印度的前瞻景氣指標卻給市場帶來了巨大的驚喜 。 最新
公布的 2026 年 5 月 HSBC 印度綜合採購經理人指數由 4 月的 58.2 逆勢回升至 59.3，創下近
6 個月來的最高擴張紀錄。其中，服務業 PMI 由 58.8 顯著彈升至 59.8，主因是國內消費模式在
中東局勢干擾下成功「外轉內」 ，內需服務動能十分強勁；製造業 PMI 5 月最終值亦來到 55.0，高
於 4 月的 54.7。 雖然新出口訂單因中東衝突擴大而減速 ， 但國內基礎建設與製造業的 「預防性囤貨」

需求，成功支撐製造業維持在歷史平均水準之上。
然而，內需並無法完全沖刷外部供給側帶來的窒息感。印度高達 85% 的原油依賴進口，中東
戰事導致國際油價攀升至每桶近 120 美元，對印度財政與物價造成極大考驗。最新數據顯示，5 月
份消費者物價指數（CPI）年增率由 4 月的 3.5% 抬頭至 3.9%。這主要是受到汽車燃油價格凍結
政策結束的影響 。此外 ，比原油更長遠的隱憂來自於氣候 。 氣象機構預警有高機率出現乾旱 。 歷史上，
降雨不足與聖嬰現象會嚴重抑制糧食與作物產量 ， 豆類、 油籽等作物的受災風險顯著高於有灌溉保護
的水稻和小麥。彭博與野村等機構一致預警，這項氣候乾旱威脅將在 10 月農作物收成期引爆新一
輪食品通膨，預估將食品與飲料通膨推向 6.0%，並使下半年整體 CPI 衝破央行的容忍上限。
面對經常帳赤字擴大、國際收支（BOP）惡化與盧比貶值壓力，印度當局並未坐以待斃。首先
在貨幣政策方面，印度央行（RBI）於 6 月 5 日一致決議維持政策回購利率在 5.25% 不變，立場

維持「中性」 。央行認為盲目升息防禦匯率不僅無效，反而可能將能源衝擊轉化為內需重傷，因此決
策者選擇保留政策空間以依賴數據靈活決策。不過，經濟學家多數預期，隨著年底通膨風險逐漸壓過
成長擔憂，央行這項「按兵不動」的政策不會持續太久。 其次在財政工具方面，為保障佔人口半數
的農業人口並穩定農村經濟，政府宣布 2026/27 財年肥料補貼預算大增 20%，並階段性減免汽柴
油，支撐內需經濟。
印度市場目前正呈現明顯的「基本面強韌」與「評價面修正」的矛盾訊號。不可否認，5 月高
企的 PMI 與 1Q GDP 的暴衝證實其內部需求並未崩潰，而政府靈活的黃金關稅政策亦成功控制了
經常帳的惡化 。 然而 ， 中東戰事帶來的原油窒息感與下半年季風降雨不足 ， 是極難迴避的供給側利空 。
這兩項毒素預計將在第四季將通膨推向 6% 以上，進而逼迫央行轉為年底升息，更高的借貸成本屆
時將進一步抑制工業與內需需求，也讓股市有評價修正的壓力。因此，我們建議現階段應採取較為防
守的中長線投資策略，以在估值向下修正的震盪期中，鎖定長期結構性獲利。

拉美市場：巴西選舉風險與通膨預期升溫，後市應保守看待
6 月以來拉美市場主軸已由單純降息利多，轉為政治風險、通膨預期與央行動態三方拉扯。巴西
總統大選民調顯示魯拉領先幅度擴大，疊加巴西通膨預期與 Selic 利率路徑連續上修，使市場開始重
新評估 2026 年政策與財政不確定性，也使整體拉美股市表現持續承壓。
選舉方面，最新公布的 Genial/Quaest 民調指出，魯拉對波索納洛的領先幅度再度擴大，第一輪
由前次 39%對 33%，轉為本次 39%對 29%，魯拉領先差距升至 10 個百分點；第二輪亦由前次魯拉
42%對波索納洛 41%，轉為本次 44%對 38%，領先 6 個百分點。由於魯拉為較不受資本市場所青睞

的候選人，其當選可能性上升，預期將不利於巴西股市表現。
另一方面 ， 通膨預期惡化也使巴西降息交易持續降溫 。截至 6 月 15 日的巴西央行市場調查指出，
2026 年巴西通膨預期由 5.11%上修 19 個基點至 5.30%，持續高於 4.50%通膨目標區間上緣。2027
至 2029 年通膨預期亦全數高於 3.0%目標，顯示市場對通膨回到央行目標區間信心不足。
受此影響，市場對巴西政策利率路徑也持續轉鷹，2026、2027、2028 年底 Selic 利率預期分別
升至 13.75%、12.00%、10.25%，較前次的 13.50%、11.50%、10.00%再度上修。財政面同樣削弱
政策可信度，2026 至 2028 年基本財政餘額預期仍為 GDP 的-0.50%、-0.40%、-0.24%，名目財政
赤字 2026 至 2029 年亦維持超過 GDP 的 7%，顯示巴西財政赤字問題持續惡化。
貨幣政策方面，巴西央行 6 月 17 日全票決議降息 25 個基點，將 Selic 利率降至 14.25%，表面

上延續降息循環 ， 但其聲明與前瞻指引缺乏清晰度 ， 使市場更難判斷央行是否會對通膨預期重新升溫
採取足夠強硬態度。巴西央行通膨預測亦同步上修，2026 年底整體通膨預測由 4.6%升至 5.2%，自
由價格通膨預測在 2026 年第四季由 4.5%升至 5.3%，在通膨壓力持續升高的狀況下，巴西短期內進
一步降息空間已相當有限。
巴西以外拉美央行亦呈現類似政策兩難，智利央行 6 月 16 日宣布維持政策利率 4.5%不變 ， 即使
其 2026 年 GDP 成長預測降至 1%至 1.75%，但仍因油價與地緣風險推升短期通膨而選擇觀望。
展望後市，拉美市場在巴西大選風險升溫、通膨預期持續攀升的背景下，後市股市上行空間料將
受限，尤其全球股市近期受到 AI 題材帶動，拉美市場在缺乏成長敘事下，表現料將難以跟上全球指
數表現，故我們認為目前對拉美市場應保守看待。

科技類股：Token 經濟學與邊緣 AI 外溢效應，帶動科技股進一步走揚
當前全球科技股正處於由 AI 算力基礎設施引導的「結構性重新定價」新起點。首先半導體產值
將正式跨越破兆美元大關，市場對於「AI 泡沫破裂」的擔憂缺乏實質數據支持。超大型雲端服務商
（CSP） 具備強韌的資產負債表與 EBITDA 創造力，大舉發債並非盲目燒錢。「Token 經濟學」 打通
了算力消耗與商業變現的阻礙 ， 這些都是科技股的中長線利多。 雖然美國貨幣政策走向可能引發短線
估值震盪，但基本面依然偏多。
根據 IDC 最新報告，全球半導體市場規模正迎來歷史性的結構性跨越，2026 年全球半導體總營

收預估將達到 1.29 兆美元 ， 較 2025 年增 52.8%。 其中 ， 單是資料中心半導體營收就將達到 4,771
億美元，預計到 2030 年將達 8,432 億美元，獨佔市場半壁江山。2026 年全球記憶體營收暴增至
5,947 億美元，其中 DRAM 營收跳升至 4,186 億美元，年增率高達 177%。主因是 CSP 業者為
了搶奪 HBM 與高階 DDR 產能，願意支付極高溢價，推升整體板塊毛利率。
輝達執行長黃仁勳定調的 「Token 經濟學」 ，將技術敘事轉化為可量化的財務獲利模型，其核心
論點為 「Compute is revenue（運算就是收入） ，AI 工廠生產的實體商品就是 Token。 而產生Token
的數量就可以與資本開支互相匹配，進而由算力推估出資本開支的回報率。在 1GW 功耗限制下，
Blackwell 世代年營收約 300 億美元；進入次世代 Vera Rubin 晶片架構後，因運算密度拉升，相
同功耗下的年營收預估將暴增 5 倍至 1,500 億美元 ， 提供 CSP 業者持續加大資本支出的實質回報

率支持。
此外，隨著集中式雲端算力的建置跨過臨界點，AI 算力紅利正加速向終端裝置外溢。智慧型手機
與 PC 本地執行 AI 推論的需求已迎來全面爆發，Edge AI 正為半導體市場創造出全新的高價值需
求類別。邊緣 AI 擺脫了過往智慧終端僅比較出貨量的殺價競爭，轉向「高附加價值」的規格升級溢
價。長期而言，隨著 AI 代理（Agentic AI）在終端裝置的滲透率攀升，半導體將迎來新一波結構性
定價與穩健的增長動能。
短期來看，由於通膨數據具備黏性，市場正重新評估聯準會 （Fed） 貨幣政策再度升息的可能性。
此流動性緊縮預期直接推升了美債無風險利率 ，可能對高估值的科技權值股造成本益比階段性震盪修
正。然而，科技巨頭極其強韌的實質獲利能力足以消化估值衝擊。2026 年半導體跨越 1.29 兆美元
產值已成剛需，CSP 業者 EBITDA 預估增長 13%，配合黃仁勳 Token 經濟學的變現邏輯，獲利增
長將有效消化高本益比，建議可持續加碼科技類股。

能源類股：荷莫茲海峽運量逐步恢復，油價料將承壓
受到美伊戰爭持續朝收斂方向前進影響，市場重新評估波斯灣出口恢復後的供給回補壓力；同
時，近期中國原油需求與進口動能明顯轉弱，使油市主軸由短線斷供風險，轉向中期供給過剩與需求
破壞，受此影響，6 月油價出現大幅回落，相關能源類股表現也出現承壓。
高盛報告指出，自美伊 6 月 17 日達成臨時協議後，荷莫茲海峽與波斯灣出口流量快速回升，中
東局勢於 6 月 27 日油輪襲擊後再度升溫前，波斯灣石油流量七日均值已達每日 1,800 萬桶，約為戰
前水準近 80%。其中荷莫茲海峽流量在協議後前 10 日增加每日 700 萬桶，6 月 24 日單日估算流量

一度達每日 1,300 萬桶 ， 約為正常水準60%； 若依過去兩週平均每日增加100 萬桶的恢復速度推估，
波斯灣流量最快可在 7 月初回到戰前每日 2,300 萬桶水準。不過，6 月 27 日油輪襲擊後，多艘超大
型油輪傳出掉頭返航 ，6 月 27 日至 6 月 28 日前後經荷莫茲海峽出口量一度降至每日 500 萬桶以下 ，
不到正常水準 20%， 顯示中東石油出口恢復方向雖明確 ， 但短期內仍取決於船東對海峽安全的信心。
另一方面，需求端壓力也有望加速全球石油供需平衡。IEA（國際能源署）6 月月報估計，第二
季全球石油供應缺口為每日 310 萬桶 ， 低於高盛估計的每日 500 萬桶 ， 主因 IEA 對石油需求破壞
的估計更高，IEA 預估第二季需求破壞量為每日 550 萬桶，高於高盛估計的每日 490 萬桶。需求
端疲弱原因主要來自中國需求下滑。彭博分析指出，中國 5 月原油需求急劇下降，原油進口量降至
每日 680 萬桶，較去年同期下滑 40%，主因包含荷莫茲海峽關閉期間造成的採購干擾，以及經濟

放緩帶來的需求破壞。進一步看至 2027 年，IEA 預估在供給年增每日 790 萬桶，以及部分需求疲
弱延續下，全球每日將出現 500 萬桶供給過剩，高於高盛預估的每日 320 萬桶，顯示中期油市風
險已由短缺轉向過剩。
受到整體油市轉往供給過剩影響，高盛將 2026 年第四季布蘭特原油價格預測由每桶 90 美元下
修至 80 美元，2027 年平均價格由 80 美元下修至 75 美元；西德州原油方面，2026 年第四季均價
預估由 85 美元下修至 75 美元，2027 年則由 75 美元下修至 70 美元。摩根大通、巴克萊、摩根士
丹利、瑞銀與滙豐也紛紛下修今明兩年油價展望，預估 2027 年油價均價介於每桶 63 至 85 美元；
其中巴克萊預期第四季可能出現每日 300 萬桶供應過剩，滙豐則預期短期將出現小型供應過剩。
展望後市，能源類股仍需觀察美伊臨時備忘錄落實狀況、荷莫茲海峽運輸恢復及中東增產狀況是
否順暢，然而當前市場更為關注停戰帶來的風險溢價收斂，故中短期內油價表現料將較為疲軟，在此

狀況下 ， 能源類股表現料將難以勝過由 AI 題材帶動的全球指數表現 ，故對後市我們亦維持保守看法。

黃金類股：央行增持黃金趨勢未變，惟聯準會偏鷹不利金價表現
6 月以來黃金類股表現持續落後整體指數表現，主因受到美國聯準會態度偏鷹派影響，市場升息
預期上升，推升美元與美債殖利率走強，連帶提高黃金這類無孳息資產的機會成本，使金價與黃金類
股短線表現持續承壓。
6 月 FOMC 會議雖維持聯邦基準利率在 3.5%至 3.75%不變，但利率點陣圖中位數由 3 月時預估
2026 年底的 3.375%上修至 3.75%，同時 18 位提交預測委員中，有 9 位預期年內至少升息一次，8
位預期維持不變，僅 1 位預期降息 1 碼，導致會後市場升息預期明顯攀升。對黃金而言，升息預期

升溫將推升美元與美債殖利率 ， 並提高持有無息資產的機會成本 ， 尤其金價先前在美伊戰爭導致長天
期公債殖利率攀升的影響下，表現已相對疲弱，近期利率預期轉鷹更容易使短線獲利了結壓力升高。
另一方面，新任聯準會主席 Kevin Warsh 在本次會議中並未屈從政治面的降息壓力，反而重申
2%通膨目標並強調貨幣政策的獨立性，有助於強化市場對聯準會政策紀律的信心。此舉雖有助於提
升美國政策可信度，卻也將對黃金帶來不利影響；由於黃金的部分避險買盤，源自於市場對美國財政
負擔、政策獨立性及美元信用的疑慮，故一旦市場相信聯準會將維持堅定對抗通膨的立場，這類避險
需求便可能逐步減弱，也讓黃金類股失去美國政策性避險這項題材。
然而，儘管短線黃金逆風重重，但是在央行買盤方面，仍未見到全球央行對於增持黃金的看法有
所轉變。世界黃金協會所出版的《2026 年央行黃金儲備調查》顯示，89%受訪央行預期未來 12 個
月全球央行黃金儲備將增加 ， 雖低於2025 年的 95%， 但仍高於2024 年的 81%與 2023 年的 71%，

顯示多數國家央行對於持續增持黃金儲備仍具高度共識。就自身配置而言， 45%受訪央行預期未來
12 個月自身黃金儲備將增加，高於 2025 年的 43%與 2024 年的 29%，並創下 2019 年以來最高比
例，另有 54%預期維持不變，僅 1%預期下降，顯示各國央行黃金配置意願尚未出現明確轉向 態勢。
該項調查也指出，各國央行增持黃金的原因，主要仍集中在儲備多元化與風險分散。90%受訪央
行認為黃金在危機時期具有避險性 ，84%認為黃金具長期保值與通膨避險功能 ，83%認為黃金是有效
的投資組合分散工具，80%認為黃金屬於儲備多元化政策的一部分。由此可見，央行買金並非單純追
逐短線價格，而是與官方儲備安全性、幣別分散及危機應對需求有關。
展望後市，全球央行增持黃金的趨勢仍未改變，然而短線上，在降息利多消失、美元及美債殖利
率偏向上行，且聯準會獨立性疑慮降溫的背景下，金價表現料將受到壓抑，預期後市黃金類股表現將
於區間內震盪整理，目前對黃金類股後市中性看待。

### Page 12

不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：市場觀望華許首秀，美債利率料陷整理走勢
6 月中的 FOMC 會議以 12–0 一致決議維持聯邦資金利率目標區間於 3.50%–3.75%， 並重申維
持充足準備金政策。然而，投資人所真正關注的內容在於會後所公布的經濟預測摘要(SEP)。在 6 月
點陣圖中，九位官員預計今年至少會升息 1 碼，其中六位預計至少升息 2 次。另有九位官員預計利
率維持不變或會降息 。2026 年底聯邦基金利率中位數由 3 月的 3.4% 上修到 3.8%，2027 年由 3.1%
上修到 3.6%；同時，2026 年 PCE 通膨預估由 2.7% 大幅上修至 3.6%，核心 PCE 由 2.7% 上修

至 3.3%。這代表 Fed 不只承認通膨反彈，也認為利率路徑需要重新往上定價。彭博對於 Fed 主席
開場談話的 NLP 情緒模型亦反映了鷹派傾向的變化。
華許在此次的會後記者會中雖重申聯準會對物價穩定與最大化就業雙重使命的承諾 ， 但針對已持
續五年多高於 2%目標的通膨問題，他強調委員會將毫不含糊地致力於恢復物價穩定。同時Fed 也將
維持銀行系統的充足準備金政策。他也指出，儘管面臨不確定性，目前經濟活動穩步擴張，生產力和
資本投資強勁，且就業市場保持穩定；本次會議發布了更簡短直接的政策聲明，取消了華許認為不合
時宜的 「前瞻性指引」 ，使得此次會後聲明用字大幅精簡 （由4/29 會議時的 341 字減至 130 字） ，
Warsh 本人也選擇不參與提交經濟預測摘要（SEP）。作為領導層上任後的重要革新，他也宣布在未
來兩週內正式成立五個獨立小組以分別對「溝通」、「資產負債表」、「數據來源」、「生產力／就
業」與「通膨框架」等議題進行改革，旨在打造一個適應時代需求且專注於未來的聯準會。

美國總體經濟環境現處於「經濟具韌性但通膨僵固」的微妙平衡。即便 FOMC 6 月會議立場偏
向鷹派，但仍約有半數委員認為年底前利率將維持現行水準。彭博指出，倘美伊停火協議得以維持，
通膨或於未來數月回落，部分 6 月預期升息的委員 9 月可望轉為按兵不動。元大投顧認為「年底前
維持利率不變仍是大概率事件」 ，因近期震盪部分是因市場「尚未適應 Warsh 新溝通風格」所產生
的過度反應。此外，高盛在 FOMC 會後發表的最新報告也指出即便  Fed「升息的可能性雖增加，
但仍非其基本預期」。
我們認為，當前美債已充分反映通膨上行風險，並提供高於過往均值的高殖利率，將可相當程度
抵銷殖利率進一步走高之風險。然因投資人對 Fed 的後續貨幣政策存有疑慮，或導致殖利率曲線短
端受升息預期變化而有較大波動；長端又有期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供給增加等風險，故
存續期過長亦非明智之舉。因此，建議債券佈局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投等債，不僅能提供
相對具吸引力的息收，若未來通膨預期隨中東緊張情勢放緩，在資本利得方面亦可望有所表現。

3Q26 result overview 
ITEQ’ 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 83/31% above our 
estimates/consensus as GM was 10.6/5.8ppt higher than 
GSe/consensus. We believe the higher than expected GM expansion 
this quarter (+12.3ppt QoQ, +20.6ppt YoY) was mainly driven by the 
higher-than-expected price hike on its low-end CCL products (M6 
grade CCL and below).  
For earnings, ITEQ 3Q26 EPS was 123%/38% higher than GSe/BBG 
consensus on the solid GM expansion. 
Valuation 
We maintain our Sell rating with a 12m TP of NT$134, based on 
13.5x 4Q26-3Q27E P/E, which is +0.5x STDV lower than past 
10-year average valuation.

### Page 3

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

ITEQ is a key high-end CCL player, mainly focused on the server market (15-20% global 
share as of 2024). While we are positive on its long-term outlook given promising growth 
in the server CCL and automotive high-speed CCL markets, we continue to see a weaker 
than peer revenue/profitability outlook, considering the company’s lagging technology. 
Further, we believe the mid- to low-end CCL market oversupply issue will continue in the 
coming years, weighing on ITEQ’s profitability in the foreseeable future. Meanwhile, the 
company is trading at a 2026E P/E that is much higher than the average peak valuation 
and Taiwan peers. We are Sell rated on the stock. Key upside risks: (1) 
stronger-than-expected demand from server/switch segments in the coming years; (2) 
stronger-than-expected CCL pricing hikes in the coming quarters without component 
cost increases; and (3) stronger-than-expected 5G BTS demand globally in the coming 
quarters.   
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$134 is based on a 4Q26E-3Q27E P/E 
multiple of 13.5x (-0.5x s.d. lower than past 10-year average valuation). 
Key upside risks: (1) stronger-than-expected demand from server/switch segments in 
the coming years; (2) stronger-than-expected CCL pricing hikes in the coming quarters 
without component cost increases; and (3) stronger-than-expected 5G BTS demand 
globally in the coming quarters.

and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following our results memo on Vanguard International (VIS), 
we raise our forecasts and 12M TP and upgrade our rating one notch to 
Buy (1), given a widened disconnect between strengthened fundamentals 
yet weakened valuations. Assessed in our Asia Foundry report, VIS’s wafer 
loadings should approach full through 2027, thanks to demand spillover 
from AI scaling augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation, with better ASP 
adding to growth. While the equity markets appear to be haunted by the US 
mid-term elections, management sees it as a “healthy wakeup call” to 
anyone with overheated AI expectations; we concur.

What’s the impact: Forecast revisions. Since operating metrics at VIS 
look broadly intact except for higher ASP as some customers’ previous 
long-term agreements expired later, we only finetune our forecasts by 
increasing our 2026-28E EPS 2-6%. We stick with our view that VIS will 
enjoy counter-seasonal prints through 2027 with sustained strong earnings 
growth, thanks again to a paradigm shift we’ve observed in the specialty 
foundry market benefiting VIS, aided by its fast 12” capacity ramp.

A healthy wake-up call? We were impressed by management’s 
commentary about potential impact from the US mid-term elections that it 
should cool down any overheated expectations for the AI buildouts in the 
markets, since deployments among AI factories, power infrastructure, 
environment and social welfare need to be reviewed and optimised for a 
right balance. That said, management views this as a healthy wake-up call 
to the markets since AI has walked in our daily lives, becoming a 
fundamental demand for an unreversed secular trend. We believe its 
commentary supports our view calling for likely a soft-landing 2028 when 
hyperscalers’ investments slow down and new supply catches up to help 
discover inflated demand, fulfil unmet demand and manage excess 
inventory for a healthy growth and balance between AI and non-AI 
consumer ecosystems. Though the correction could be painful, the equity 
markets appear to have discounted this risk now than later, which looks to 
set a healthy wakeup call to the equity market as well, in our view.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we trim our 12M TP 
for VIS to TWD200 (previous: TWD210), on our lower ROE-adjusted target 
PBR of 5x (previous: 5.2x) pegging to our lowered target multiple for TSMC 
(2330 TT, TWD2,235, Buy [1]) That said, we upgrade our rating one notch 
to Buy (1) on valuation grounds with key downside risks of the current chip 
upturn ending earlier and 12” ramp/execution coming in below expectation.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 3-11% above the consensus 
forecasts likely due to our higher trajectory of ASP assumptions.

9 November 2026

Upgrading: a healthy reset in expectations

➢ Fine-tuning up our forecasts after the results call 
➢ A healthy wakeup call from the US mid-term elections? 
➢ Upgrading rating to Buy (1) with a revised 12M TP of TWD200

Company profile 
Founded in December 1994 with stocks listed in March 1998, Vanguard International Semiconductor 
Corp. (VIS) successfully transformed its business model from memory to complementary metal oxide 
semiconductor (CMOS) logic foundry focusing on specialty technologies such as mixed signal, power 
analog and embedded memory, growing as one leading specialty foundry in the world. Leveraging a 
strategic relationship with its major shareholder TSMC, VIS operates wafer fabs in Taiwan and 
Singapore, servicing a high variety of customers including fabless chipmakers and IDMs around the 
world.

ESG analysis

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
VIS's board of directors (BoD) consists of 9 members, including 5 independent directors,
representing 56% of the total board seats. The board comprises members with diverse
backgrounds in business management, finance, industry, and academia, providing a broad
range of expertise and perspectives.
We believe the majority-independent board structure and the directors’ collective experience in
leadership, corporate governance, industry expertise, financial management, risk management,
and sustainability strengthen the effectiveness of the BoD and support VIS's long-term growth
and corporate governance practices.
Capital management 1 VIS pays its dividend on a quarterly basis. In the past 5 years, it has managed its dividend payout well, as evidenced by its dividend in dollar terms, where it has never lowered its dividend.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were around 1-2% of its total revenue each year. The percentage is insignificant and thus we see limited risk for VIS.
S Supply chain management 1
VIS has established a comprehensive sustainable supply chain management framework to
ensure suppliers comply with its sustainability requirements covering environmental protection,
labor and human rights, occupational health and safety, business ethics, and responsible
sourcing. The company requires suppliers to adhere to its "VIS Supplier Code of Conduct" and
"Corporate Sustainability Policy", and conducts supplier sustainability assessments and audits
as part of its supplier management process. We believe these efforts strengthen VIS's supply
chain resilience and help mitigate supply chain risks.
E Water & wastewater management 2
VIS is committed to improving water efficiency and sustainable water management. In 2022, the
company implemented the ISO 46001 Water Efficiency Management System to analyze water
consumption patterns, identify significant water-use equipment, evaluate water-saving
opportunities, and establish improvement measures. VIS also provides annual water
management training at its Taiwan facilities to promote continuous improvement in water-use
efficiency. We believe these efforts demonstrate the company's commitment to responsible
water stewardship and help reduce operational risks associated with water resources.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
VIS adopts a comprehensive resource management approach to waste management and has
established waste management procedures in accordance with ISO 14001 standards. Through
dedicated waste management personnel and regular employee training, the company promotes
effective waste reduction, classification, collection, storage, and treatment practices across its
operations.
E GHG emissions 1
VIS adopts a source-segregation and multi-stage treatment approach, supported by high-
efficiency air pollution control systems, to effectively manage VOC and acidic/alkaline gas
emissions from semiconductor manufacturing processes.
The company's emission levels remain well below regulatory limits, reflecting its commitment to
environmental compliance and sustainable operations.

Asia Vital Components (3017.TW)
3Q26 Beat on Margins Reinforces AI Thermal Ramp; TP Maintained at 
NT$4,350
CITI'S TAKE 
AVC reported net profit of NT$11.6bn, topping Coto/cons estimates by 
2%/2% respectively, driven by margins beat partly offset by a non-ops FX 
loss. GPM improvement was above expectations on a richer liquid cooling 
mix, yield gains and disciplined raw material pass-through. Mgmt. guided 
positively for 4Q26 and 2027, led by AI ASIC ramp-up and Vera Rubin ramp 
up. We model 16% QoQ revenue growth for 4Q26E. GPM guidance also 
points to further sequential improvement on a higher liquid cooling mix and 
scale effects. Mgmt. reiterated 2026E/27E capex of NT$15bn/15-20bn, 
alongside 50%+ cold plate capacity expansion, underscoring capacity 
conviction. We fine-tune our earnings forecasts to reflect updated guidance 
and maintain TP at NT$4,350 (25x 2027E EPS). Reiterate Buy.
3Q26 results beat on margins — AVC’s reported 3Q26 net profit of NT$11.6bn 
(+20% QoQ/+52% YoY) beat Citi/cons forecast by 2%/2%, driven by better-than-
expected margins, partly offset by a non-ops loss. 3Q26 GPM came in at 33.9% 
(+1.3ppts QoQ/+7.7 ppts YoY), above both Citi/cons estimates, mainly due to 
continued mix improvement, a higher liquid cooling contribution, yield gains, and 
disciplined pass-through of raw material price fluctuations to customers. Non-ops 
included a NT$304mn FX loss, which partially offset the strong core business.
ASIC ramp and capacity guidance underpin long-term outlook — Mgmt. guided 
positively for 4Q26 and 2027, led by AI ASIC project ramp-up and early Vera Rubin 
generation contribution. We now model 16% QoQ revenue growth for 4Q26E. GPM 
guidance also points to further sequential improvement vs. 3Q26, driven by a higher 
liquid cooling mix and scale effects. Mgmt. expects server compute tray liquid 
cooling penetration to reach 100% by end-2027. Mgmt. reiterated 2026E/26E capex 
guidance of NT$15bn/15-20bn, alongside 50%+ cold plate capacity expansion, 
reinforcing confidence in capacity build-out.
Implications — We fine-tune our 2026E-28E earnings forecasts to reflect updated 
guidance and outlook. We maintain our TP at NT$4,350, still based on 25x 2027E EPS. 
We believe AVC’s solid supply position across key customers’ AI GPU and ASIC projects, 
combined with a sustained growth trajectory, continues to support its valuation. With 
visibility extending into 2027, we see limited near-term read-through risk from the 
ongoing platform transition. We reiterate our Buy rating on AVC.

Bull/Bear: Asia Vital Components (3017.TW)

### Page 6

Asia Vital Components (3017.TW)
10 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Asia Vital Components
Company description
Asia Vital Components (AVC), founded in 1991, is a global leader in thermal
solutions, originally focusing on PCs but now specializing in servers and
networking. With Furukawa Electric (Japan) as its largest shareholder, AVC
provides a comprehensive range of cooling components, including vapor
chambers (VCs), fans, cold plates, CDUs, and manifolds, as well as chassis

and assembly services for PCs and servers, enabling holistic thermal solution
designs and one-stop shop services. AVC's diverse product portfolio supports
various sectors, including consumer IT, cloud infrastructure, transportation,
energy and networking.
Investment strategy
We rate AVC a Buy. AVC is well-positioned for the liquid-cooling transition,
with leadership in cold plates and broad participation across Nvidia and CSP
projects. Its high-volume machining capability, manufacturing uniformity,

and Vietnam-based capacity provide it with a defensible position as
Blackwell and future deployments expand. The company captures a wider
share of the thermal BOM, across coldplates, manifolds, QDs, and CDU
opportunities, providing a multi-layered earnings engine.
Valuation
We value AVC using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum
and the expansion of its addressable market. Our TP of NT$4,350 is derived
from applying a 25x PE multiple on 2027E EPS, which we believe is justified

given the company’s entrenched positioned in the server thermal segment,
rising contribution from liquid cooling, and the increasing value of thermal
content per server as power density continues to climb. With these structural
drivers still in place, we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its
historical averages.
Risks
This stock is High Risk based upon our quantitative model but assigning a
High Risk rating is not supported by other qualitative factors such as cold-

plate leadership and deep CSP penetration, and so a High Risk rating has not
been applied. Key downside risks to our investment thesis and target price
include: 1) slower adoption pace for liquid cooling adoption; 2) heightened
price competition; 3) lower-than-expected share in liquid cooling
components; and 4) emerging alternative cooling technologies.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Hon Precision, the global leader 
in test handlers and active thermal control (ATC) systems, with a Buy (1) 
rating. According to management, the company commands >70% market 
share in AI/HPC final test (FT) and c.50% in system-level test (SLT) by 
revenue, distinctly positioning it as one of the key global beneficiaries of the 
rising silicon complexity.

Multi-layer content stacking supports ASP and margin growth. As test 
durations continue to lengthen and thermal requirements rise, customers 
are required to deploy greater parallel testing capacity and higher-
specification handlers, creating a structural demand tailwind for both FT 
and SLT handler. We forecast rising AI chip power consumption and 
package complexity over our forecast horizon to drive adoption of higher-
value ATC systems, mega-tray handlers (adding 25-30% to ASP relative to 
standard dual-temp ATC systems), and AOI integration (adding a further 
10-15% to system ASP), supporting a multi-year increase in content per 
tool and expanding its gross margin from 56.8% to 59.2% over 2026-28E.

Recurring consumables and CPO testing expand long-term growth 
visibility. Cold plates generate high-margin, annuity-like revenue with 
every new chip platform which should benefit from shortening AI processor 
refresh cycles. Beyond consumables, Hon Precision is extending its testing 
platform into co-packaged optics (CPO), where optical-electrical co-testing 
could unlock a new addressable market beyond traditional GPU and ASIC 
testing. While near-term contribution remains limited, we view CPO as a 
strategic growth option that enhances the company’s long-term earnings 
visibility and valuation premium over our forecast horizon.

Catalysts: 1) capacity expansion ramp in 2027; 2) mega-tray penetration 
and higher-power ATC adoption; 3) initial CPO test solution qualification.

Valuation: We initiate with a Buy (1) rating and 12M TP of TWD6,365, 
based on a target PER of 30x applied on our 2027E EPS. The stock 
currently trades at c.19x forward PER, despite our projected 2026-28E EPS 
CAGR of 55%, implying a PEG of only 0.35x. We believe its current 
valuation heavily discounts macro noise with share price pulling back by 
c.53% from its historical high and derating from a cycle-peak multiple of 45-
50x 1-year-forward PER. Backed by solid order visibility through late-2027, 
we highlight Hon Precision’s risk-reward profile as decisively asymmetric.

Risks: 1) Slower-than-expected AI/HPC investment; 2) delayed ramp-up of 
new products; and 3) potential loss of market share.
